MEETING: Cheyenne City Council | DATE: 2008-05-27 | CLIP_ID: 24 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 24

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
May 27, 2008
Due to the Memorial Day holiday, the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne instead met
in regular session on this date at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.  Present were: MAYOR-Jack R.
Spiker; COUNCIL MEMBERS-Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Collins, Mr. Laybourn,  Dr. Rinne
(arriving at 6:10 p.m.) and Mr. Valdez.  Absent: Ms. Case, Mr. Pierson and Mr. Segrave.
Also present: Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk; Barb Dorr, City Treasurer, and Claudia Angelos,
City Attorney.
Following the pledge of allegiance, Rusty Mellon and Keith Reichert, representatives of
Greeley’s I ndependence S tampede C ommittee, s ubmitted a nd read a p roclamation no ting t he
Stampede’s 86  anniversary of its annual celebration to be held June 25 through July 6, 2 008,th
proclaiming Friday, July 4th as Cheyenne Day, and inviting the Cheyenne community to attend the
event in Greeley, CO.
EXPRESSION OF APPRECIATION TO MAYOR’S YOUTH COUNCIL AND STATE OF
THE YOUTH REPORT.  Joseph Chenchar, Youth Council Chair, provided a State of the Youth
Report and described various projects the Youth Council participated in during the past school year,
including the proposed community recreation center, and commented on Youth Council members’
positive interactions and learning experiences through involvement with local government issues.  He
provided suggestions for future Youth Council members, and expressed appreciation to those who
provided assistance to the group. Mayor Spiker and Jan Spires, Mayor’s Youth Council coordinator,
commended members of the 2007-2008 Mayor’s Youth Council and presented each member present
with a certificate and gift of appreciation. Youth Council members recognized were: Zack Anderson,
Joseph Chenchar, Zeb Glantz, Bryant Jackson, Kelsey James, Ryan Kolb (not in attendance), Ryan
Ragaglia, Tanya Salih, Luke Seebaum, Roxanne Vigil (not in attendance), and Ashley Woods.
Consent Agenda. (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to be
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests, in which event the item
will be removed from the Consent Agenda and considered in its normal sequence on the agenda.) 
Upon request by Mr. Laybourn, Mayor Spiker announced agenda items #27, #33(a)(b)(c)(d)(f)(g),
34(a), 35(a) and 36(a) would be removed from the Consent Agenda. Mr. Collins  moved to approve
remaining consent agenda items, seconded by Mr. Brown.  M otion carried.  Voting “yes” – all
members of the governing body present with the exception of Mr. Laybourn voting “no”.
[CA] MINUTES FROM REGULAR MEETING OF THE GOVERNING BODY ON May 12,
2008.  Motion (per consent agenda) carried.
[CA] MINUTES FROM VOUCHERS MEETING OF THE GOVERNING BODY ON MAY 19,
2008.  Motion (per consent agenda) carried.
Mr. Valdez moved to approve payment of vouchers as presented, seconded by Mr. Collins.
Upon inquiry, Barb Dorr, City Treasurer, reviewed information available on a voucher payable to
Trihydro Corp. ($1,082.40), for ground water investigation at the landfill.  Motion carried.  Voting
“yes” – all members of the governing body present.

(located at the southeast quadrant of Fox Farm Rd. and Walterscheid Blvd. intersection).  Brandon

Wyo. Stat. §15-1-402, including specific information indicating compliance with Wyo. Stat. §15-1-
402(a)(i) through (vi).  There being no further comments or input and purpose of the hearing having
been met, Mayor Spiker closed the hearing.

Agreement between the City of Cheyenne, Sanitation Department, and Waste Management
Disposal Services of Colorado, Inc., for non-hazardous solid waste disposal (North Weld Landfill).
Mr. Brown moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Valdez.  Discussion followed regarding the process
utilized in reviewing options affiliated with disposal of solid waste; management of municipal waste
needs; t ransferring so lid wa ste o ut o f st ate; Lan dfill A dvisory Co mmittee’s r ecommendations;
community recycling; potential future technological solutions, and current landfill life span. Concern
was expressed with past expenditures involving potential waste-to-energy solutions, and purchase
of the Belvoir Ranch as a potential location of a new City landfill.  Upon inquiry during discussion,
Mike Abel, Public Works Director, advised approximately 47,000 tons of waste per year will be
transferred for disposal at the proposed Weld County landfill; that approximately $962,000.00 has
been budgeted for first year expenses (includes tipping fees and transportation costs), and it would
cost an estimated $40 million dollars to implement a new City landfill site.  He indicated, although
transferring o f so lid waste is not t he most attractive o ption, it is t he most r easonable and co st
effective at this time.  Discussion included: Landfill Advisory Committee’s recommendations; need
to continue seeking solid waste disposal options; future alternatives that may be available through
technology advances, and limited life span of the current landfill. Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all
members of the governing body present with the exception of Mr. Laybourn voting “no”.  (#5255)
(CA) Contract between the City of Cheyenne, Police Department, and Cheyenne Regional Airport

for their support and assistance with the project.

adjourned at 7:25 p.m.

MEETING: Cheyenne City Council | DATE: 2008-06-09 | CLIP_ID: 28 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 28

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
June 9, 2008
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date at 6:00 p.m.
in Council Chambers.  Present were: MAYOR-Jack R. Spiker; COUNCIL MEMBERS-Mr. Brown,
Ms. Broyles, Ms. Case, Mr. Collins, Mr. Laybourn, Mr. Pierson, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Segrave and Mr.
Valdez. 
Also present: Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk; Barb Dorr, City Treasurer, and Claudia Angelos,
City Attorney.
Consent Agenda. (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to be
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests, in which event the item
will be removed from the Consent Agenda and considered in its normal sequence on the agenda.) 
Upon request by Mr. Laybourn, Mayor Spiker announced agenda items #25, 26, 35(a)(b), 36(c),
37(a)(c)(d)(e) and 38(a) would be removed from the Consent Agenda.  Mr. Collins  m oved to
approve the remaining Consent Agenda items, seconded by Mr. Brown.  Motion carried.  Voting
“yes” – all members of the governing body with the exception of Mr. Laybourn voting “no”.
[CA] MINUTES FROM REGULAR MEETING OF THE GOVERNING BODY ON MAY 27,
2008.  Motion (per consent agenda) carried.
[CA] MINUTES FROM VOUCHERS MEETING OF THE GOVERNING BODY ON JUNE 2,
2008.  Motion (per consent agenda) carried.
Mr. Brown declared a conflict of interest (business interest in Ameri-Tech HVAC Services)
and vacated Council Chambers.  Mr. Valdez  moved to approve payment of vouchers as presented,
seconded by Mr. Pierson. Upon inquiry, Barb Dorr, City Treasurer, reviewed information available
for vouchers payable to Ameri-Tech HVAC Services ($11,375.00), water treatment equipment at
Ice and Events Center, and Ayers Associates, Inc. ($48.652.43), Clear Creek wetland design and Dry
Creek-Sheridan Reach Flood Control p roject. Upon inquiry r egarding t he Clear Creek w etland
design, Rick Parish, Director of Parks and Recreation Dept., provided information on the status of
the master plan for the David R. Romero South Cheyenne Community Park.  Upon further inquiry,
Ms. Dorr reviewed information available for vouchers payable to:  Cheyenne Concrete Specialists
($19,095.00), c ompletion of  c oncrete w ork a t th e A irport G olf C ourse; A lbert R . B ennett
Investigations, LL C ( $160.00), P olice Dep t. cl andestine l ab t raining, an d JP  Mo rgan Ch ase
($54,765.01), promoter proceeds for World Wrestling Entertainment event held at the Ice and Events
Center.  Upon inquiry, Ms. Dorr and Ken Lewis, City Engineer, clarified a voucher payable to Union
Pacific Railroad ( $5,000.00) w as pursuant t o Co ntract # 4505, st orm sewer c rossing for N orris
Viaduct pro ject, and n oted o ther pay ments t o UPRR  wi ll b e f orthcoming due t o co ntractual
obligations.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present in Council
Chambers with the exception of Mr. Laybourn voting “no”.  Following announcement of the vote,
Mr. Brown returned to Council Chambers.
Mayor Spiker opened a public meeting to discuss the submission of a grant application to the
Federal Transit Administration, an op erating administration of the United States Department of
Transportation, f or f ederal tr ansportation a ssistance a s a uthorized b y 4 9 U .S.C. § 5307, f or
approximately $1,083,348.00 in federal funds.  Joe Dougherty, Transit Director, explained how funds
would be utilized and local funding matches through the City, contracts and grants, and program
income, resulting in a total income amount of $1,966,993.00.  There being no further comments or
input and purpose of the public meeting having been met, Mayor Spiker closed the public meeting.
Mayor Spiker opened a public hearing to elicit public comment on a proposed First Mortgage
Revenue Bond to be issued by Laramie County, WY, in an amount not to exceed $4,150,000.00 on
behalf of  P eak W ellness C enter, I nc., a  W yoming n onprofit c orporation, f or th e purpo se of
refinancing an outstanding loan, providing funds for additional remodeling and furnishing of a facility
located at 510 W. 29  St., Cheyenne, WY, for use as a clinic to house the Youth Services Divisionth
and administrative offices for Peak Wellness Center, Inc., and paying certain costs incidental to the
authorization, issuance and sale of the bond.  Barbara Bonds, Freudenthal and Bonds PC (law firm),
reviewed pas t act ions b efore t he go verning b ody rel ative t o t he pro posed b ond an d ex plained
circumstances that have delayed the project. She advised although the County, not the City, will be
the bond issuer, the Internal Revenue Service requires the City, as the primary governmental entity
having jurisdiction over the area, be notified of and provide approval for issuance of the bond. She
reviewed the status of bond actions before the County Commissioners and clarified a proposed City
resolution does not obligate the City legally or financially.  Dr. David Birney, Executive Director,
Peak Wellness Center, reviewed the project generally and stated bond issuance will allow Peak to
expand and provide more services to the community. Upon inquiry, Mr. Birney advised demand for
community youth services continues to increase, and discussed issues affecting services. There being
no further comments or input and purpose of the public hearing having been met, Mayor Spiker
closed the public hearing.
Mayor Spiker opened a public meeting to discuss the Cheyenne Transit Program’s boarding
transition to signed “Designated Boarding Areas,” amending the fixed routes of Cheyenne transit
buses, and recognizing the Five-Year Transportation Development Plan, as a flexible planning tool.
Joe Dougherty, Transit Director, provided a report on the updated routes and changes to the routes
involving a t ransition to designated boarding areas. He explained that currently Transit utilizes a
“wide open boarding” system which allows individuals to board the bus anywhere along the route that
is determined that safe boarding could be provided, and stated as the system has grown there have
become more and more demands for stops.  Mr. Dougherty reviewed a map of Transit service areas,
proposed changes, routes and number of designated boarding areas assigned to each route, and stated
the proposed changes are posted on the City’s website and in various community locations for the
public’s information.  H e noted the proposed changes are still o pen to r ecommendations. Upon
inquiry, Mr. Dougherty provided information on curb-to-curb service for individuals with special
needs, potential future pilot programs, number of diesel vs. gasoline buses in service, and ridership
levels and affiliated costs.  T here being no further comments or input and purpose of the public
meeting having been met, Mayor Spiker closed the public meeting.

CONSIDERATION OF BIDS:
 Consideration of Bid #M-15-08 for a contract between the City of Cheyenne and Tarco, Inc.
(Arvada, CO), for the Dry Creek-Sheridan Reach Flood Control Improvement Project (2007-2010
1% Sales Tax). Mr. Segrave moved to accept the bid submitted by Tarco, Inc. in an amount not to
exceed $3,929,611.00 (includes Alternate #1), seconded by Mr. Valdez.  U pon inquiry, Nat han
Beauheim, Traffic Engineer, advised the proposed work location involving Alternate #1 is off of
Windmill Rd. a pproximately 200-300 ft. north o f Prairie V iew G olf Course, a nd stated t he bid
includes installing a pedestrian underpass underneath Windmill Rd. for the Greenway system and
connecting it to the existing trail to the west; bid includes lighting.  Upon inquiry, Jeff Wiggins, Parks
and Recreation Dept., noted there currently is no funding in place to e xtend the Greenway path
eastward, h owever, pl anning i s un derway to do so.  He e xplained t he co ntract pro vides an
opportunity for cost savings by doing the underpass work in conjunction with road work for re-
alignment for the drainage way.  City Treasurer Barb Dorr verified project funding is from the current
5th penny 1% sales tax.  In response to concern expressed with past flooding conditions at Prairie
View Golf Course due to airport runway water runoff, Mr. Beauheim stated the drainage project will
assist in capturing and conveying runoff water into the creek.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all
members of the governing body.  (#5260)
Consideration of Bid #M-30-08 for a contract between the City of Cheyenne and Northstar
Concrete, Inc. (Loveland, CO), for the East Cheyenne Greenway Phase IIIa Project (1% Specific
Purpose Sales & Use Tax).  Dan Hawkins, Knife River, requested a review of the bid documents,
expressing concern that the proposed successful bidder submitted incomplete bid information on sub-
contractor percentages.  Rudy Apodaca, Purchasing Director, reviewed bid requirements; explained
disclosure of sub-contractor percentages is mostly used in determining the 5% in-state preference
(pursuant to state law) for Wyoming bidders, and provided examples of other bids approved involving
non-completion of sub-contractor percentages.  He advised the City reserves the right to reject any
or all proposals or waive any formalities or irregularities in proposals received.  Upon inquiry, he
stated his recommendation would be to move forward with the low bid received as lack of disclosure
of the percentages by the proposed successful bidder does not warrant denial of the bid submitted.
Jeannie Gebhart, Knife River, questioned items included in the bid document form but not required
to be completed.  Mr. Segrave advised there was no recommendation from Finance Committee.  Mr.
Segrave moved to accept the bid received from Northstar Concrete, Inc. in an amount not to exceed
$185,757.00, seco nded b y Mr.  Br own.  Di scussion f ollowed o n b id d ocument r equirements,
clarification o f document w ording f or f uture b ids, s etting a  p recedent, a nd s ub-contractor
percentages.  In response to query concerning the Greenway project, Jeff Wiggins, Parks &
Recreation Dept., provided information on the proposed path alignment from 12  & College to theth
Henderson drainage area, drainage area ownership and public access areas. Discussion followed on
the low bid received with the next lowest bid reflecting an increased cost of approximately $40,000,
preferential treatment, in-state vs. out-of-state bidders, and bid documents lacking sub-contractor
percentage figures.  Mr. Apodaca confirmed the issue involving sub-contractor percentages is a moot
point relative to the agenda item as the percentages are only important if a Wyoming firm is involved
as a bidder and out-of-state sub-contractors to be used exceed 30%. Motion carried. Voting “yes”
– all members of the governing body with the exception of Ms. Case, Mr. Laybourn and  Dr. Rinne
voting “no.” (#5261)
Consideration of Bid #R-29-08 for furnishing eleven new 2008 Ford Crown Victorias, from
Greiner Ford (Casper) and three new 2008 Ford Expeditions with police package from Laramie Peak
Motors (Laramie) for the Police Department (2007-2010 1% Sales Tax).  Mr. Segrave moved to
accept the bids submitted by Greiner Ford, in an amount not to exceed $249,590.00, and Laramie
Peak Motors, in an amount not t o exceed $73, 964.46, for a t otal bid amount o f $323, 554.46,
seconded by Mr. Valdez.  Upon inquiry, Rob Korber, Police Department, provided information on
staff assigned vehicles, mileage, and value of vehicles as tr ade-ins vs. disposal through the City
auction.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the exception of
Dr. Rinne voting “no.”

equipment from Wireless Advanced Communications, Inc., Evans, CO, for the Police Department
(2007-2010 1% S ales Tax).  M r. Segrave moved t o accept t he bid in an amount not t o exceed
$148,156.00, seconded by Mr. Brown.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing
body. 
Consideration of Bid #R-33-08 for furnishing eighteen new Panasonic Toughbook laptop
computers for the Fire & Rescue Department (2003-2006 1% Sales Tax).  Mr. Segrave moved to
accept the bid submitted by CDW-Government, Inc., Veron Hills, IL, in an amount not to exceed

funding is in conjunction with a Fire Act grant and the new fire records management system to be
implemented.  Upon inquiry, Theresa Snyder, Information Technology Director, advised the laptops
are “hard and tough”, reinforced, and are co nstructed to be extremely durable.  Motion carried.
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.

OTHER CITY BUSINESS:
John Fafautakis complimented Cheyenne Symphony Orchestra for providing local students
with qu ality concert o pportunities, an d ex pressed co ncerns wi th t he Ci ty’s b id r esponse
documentation relative to action taken earlier in the meeting.
Rudy Ap odaca, P urchasing D irector, a dvised e ffective J uly 1 , 2 008, p ursuant t o r ecent
legislative changes to s tate statute, the threshhold for requiring the bid process will change from
$20,000.00 to $35,000.00. Concern was expressed regarding the increased amount.
Pursuant to inquiry concerning status of the issuance of bids for the Deming-Walterscheid
reconstruction project, Ken Lewis, City Engineer, provided project information, indicating final plans
are being reviewed and the project should be advertised for bids in the near future, and explained
circumstances involving determination of bid response and construction time frames. Upon further
inquiry, Mr. Lewis reviewed the status of the proposed Carey Ave. extension project with regard to
the local Gunslinger’s seasonal performance (Old Town Mall) area, advising the project is designed
but a funding source has yet to be designated prior to seeking governing body approval for the
project. Upon further inquiry later in the meeting, Mr. Lewis and Mayor Spiker provided information
on an ticipated pro ject co sts an d s taff co mmunication wi th t he Gu nslingers gro up o n o ptional
performance sites.
Upon i nquiry, M ike A bel, Pub lic W orks Di rector, pro vided i nformation o n do wntown
concrete repairs and work time frame, and stated the budget for the program is funded through 5th
Penny Sales Tax.
Upon inquiry regarding information on the City’s cash reserves, City Treasurer Barb Dorr
advised she woul d provide information on the amount of reserves prior to t he Special (budget)
Session, indicating she wished to provide the most accurate and up-to-date information possible.
Following comments, Ms. Dorr stated she would compile the information as soon as possible.
There being no further business to come before the governing body, the meeting was officially
adjourned at 8:28 p.m.

MEETING: Cheyenne City Council | DATE: 2008-06-23 | CLIP_ID: 31 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 31

 RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
June 23, 2008
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date at 6:00 p.m.
in Council Chambers.  Present were: MAYOR-Jack R. Spiker; COUNCIL MEMBERS-Mr. Brown,
Ms. Case, Mr. Collins, Mr. Laybourn, Mr. Pierson, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Segrave and Mr. Valdez.  Absent:
Ms. Broyles.
 
Also present: Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk; Barb Dorr, City Treasurer, and Claudia Angelos,
City Attorney.
Consent Agenda. (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to be
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests, in which event the item
will be removed from the Consent Agenda and considered in its normal sequence on the agenda.) 
Upon request by Mr. Laybourn, Mayor Spiker announced agenda items 22, 23, 24, 25, 26, 29(a)(b)
(c)(d)(e)(f)(h), 31(a)(b)(c)(d)(e)(f), and 32(a)(b) would be removed from the Consent Agenda. Mr.
Collins moved to a pprove remaining Consent Agenda items, seconded by Mr. Brown.  M otion
carried.  V oting “yes” –  a ll members o f t he g overning body p resent w ith t he e xception o f Mr.
Laybourn voting “no”.
[CA] MINUTES FROM REGULAR MEETING OF THE GOVERNING BODY ON JUNE 9,
2008.  Motion (per consent agenda) carried.
[CA] MINUTES FROM VOUCHERS MEETING OF THE GOVERNING BODY ON JUNE 16,
2008.  Motion (per consent agenda) carried.
[CA] MINUTES FROM SPECIAL SESSION OF THE GOVERNING BODY ON J UNE 16,
2008.  Motion (per consent agenda) carried.
Mr. Segrave  moved to approve payment of vouchers as presented, seconded by Mr. Valdez.
Mr. Brown moved to divide the question to allow for a separate vote on Ameri-Tech HVAC Services
vouchers, seconded by Mr. Pierson.  M otion t o divide t he question carried.  V oting “yes” – all
members of the governing body present with the exception of Mr. Laybourn voting “no”.  Mr. Brown
declared a conflict of interest (business interest in Ameri-Tech HVAC Services) and vacated Council
Chambers. Mr. Segrave moved to approve the vouchers payable to Ameri-Tech HVAC Services
($1,036.80, float valve/copper float; $85.00, quarterly maintenance billing), seconded by Mr. Pierson.
Upon inquiry, Barb Dorr, City Treasurer, advised the work location involving the copper float/valve
was the Taco John’s Ice & Events Center, and the $85.00 voucher was a carry-over from the June
16th voucher meeting. Motion to approve Ameri-Tech HVAC Services vouchers carried.  Voting
“yes” – all members of the governing body present in Council Chambers with the exception of Mr.
Laybourn voting “no”.  Following announcement of the vote, Mr. Brown returned t o C ouncil
Chambers.  Mr. Collins declared a conflict of interest regarding a voucher payable to his business
(Bicycle Station). Upon further inquiry, Ms. Dorr reviewed information available on vouchers payable
to the Board of Public Utilities (BOPU) ($3,184.39), fertilizer for Belvoir Ranch; CPS Interactive,
Inc. ($185.00), emergency telephone monitoring in City parking structure; East High School ($300),
full color ad (“Drive Smart”) in yearbook (police grant). Upon inquiry regarding the voucher payable
to East High School and whether the same ad would be placed in Central High School’s year book,
Police Chief Bob Fecht reported Central High had not contacted the department with a s imilar
request.  Main motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present with the
exception of Mr. Collins who did not vote.
Mayor Spiker opened four public hearings for the purpose of receiving comments and input
to determine whether compliance of annexation  conditions required by Wyo. Stat. §15-1-402 exists
for the following described properties:
The West 60’ of the East 120’ of the South 120’ of Tract 336, Sunnyside Addition, Seventh
Filing, Laramie County, WY.  Brandon Cammarata, Urban Planning office, reviewed requirements
and compliance of conditions contained in Wyo. Stat. § 15-1-402, including specific information
indicating compliance with Wyo. Stat. §15-1-402(a)(i) through (vi). There being no further comments
or input and purpose of the hearing having been met, Mayor Spiker closed the hearing.
The n ortherly p ortion of Lot 1 , B lock 1 , i nclusive o f t he 1 6’ u tility e asement, R ogers
Subdivision, and Lots 3 through 5, and the northerly half of adjacent vacated alley, Block 2, Coulson
Addition, Laramie County, WY.  Brandon Cammarata, Urban Planning office, reviewed requirements
and compliance of conditions contained in Wyo. Stat. § 15-1-402, including specific information
indicating compliance with Wyo. Stat. §15-1-402(a)(i) through (vi). There being no further comments
or input and purpose of the hearing having been met, Mayor Spiker closed the hearing.
Lot 16, Block 1, Do wney Addition, Laramie County, WY.  Bran don Cammarata, Urban
Planning office, reviewed requirements and compliance of conditions contained in Wyo. Stat. §15-1-
402, including specific information indicating compliance with Wyo. Stat. §15-1-402(a)(i) through
(vi). There being no further comments or input and purpose of the hearing having been met, Mayor
Spiker closed the hearing.
Public road and alley right-of-way situated in the NW¼ of Section 27, T.14N., R.66W., of
the 6  P.M., Laramie County, WY (located N. of Pershing Blvd., S. of Dell Range Blvd., E. of Groveth
Dr. and W. of College Dr.).  Brandon Cammarata, Urban Planning office, reviewed requirements and
compliance of conditions contained in Wyo. Stat. §15-1-402, including specific information indicating

“AN ORDINANCE APPROVING AND ESTABLISHING REVISED SEPTIC WASTE
DUMP RATES AND LIQUID COMMERCIAL WASTE RATES AS RECOMMENDED BY THE
BOARD OF PUBLIC UTILITIES, TO BE EFFECTIVE JANUARY 1, 2009.”  Reporting for
Finance Committee, Mr. Segrave moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr.
Brown.  Upon inquiry, Tim Wilson, BOPU Director, provided information pursuant to discussion
held at the Board’s budget meeting involving the indexes that are available to review “bench marks”
in ascertaining rate increases relative to the cost of providing services.  He advised the Consumer
Price Index was used and the proposed rates reflect a 4.1% increase.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes”
– all members of the governing body present. (#3809)
“AN ORDINANCE APPROVING AND ESTABLISHING REVISED ADMINISTRATIVE

PUBLIC UTILITIES, TO BE EFFECTIVE JANUARY 1, 2009.”  Mr. Segrave moved to approve
on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Valdez.   Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members
of the governing body present. (#3810)
“AN O RDINANCE A PPROVING A ND ESTA BLISHING REVISED TA P F EES,

1, 2009".  M r. Segrave moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Brown.

“AN ORDINANCE APPROVING AND ESTABLISHING REVISED SEWER RATES,

JANUARY 1, 2009.”  Mr. Segrave moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr.
Valdez.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present. (#3812)
“AN ORDINANCE APPROVING AND ESTABLISHING REVISED WATER RATES,
AS RECOM MENDED BY THE  BO ARD OF  PUBL IC UTI LITIES, T O B E E FFECTIVE
JANUARY 1, 2009.”  Mr. Segrave moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr.
Brown. Upon inquiry, Tim Wilson, BOPU Director, advised the proposed rate increase would reflect

gallons of water used per month).  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing
body present. (#3813)
“AN ORDINANCE AMENDING ORDINANCE NO. 3763 DEFINING THE TERMS
AND CONDITIONS OF EMPLOYMENT FOR NON-UNIFORMED EMPLOYEES OF THE

AND  C ONDITIONS OF EMPLOYMENT FOR SWORN OFFICERS OF THE CHEYENNE
POLICE DEPARTMENT.”  Mr. Segrave moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded

(#3815)

AND FOOD SERVICE ESTABLISHMENTS, OF TITLE 8, HEALTH AND SAFETY, OF THE

SAFETY RULE 2 006 ED ITION, A S A MENDED, A LSO K NOWN A S TH E C HEYENNE-
LARAMIE COUNTY FOOD RULE, INCLUDING LOCAL AMENDMENTS.”  Reporting for
Public Services Committee, Mr. Pierson moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by

PARCELS OF LAND DESCRIBED AS TRACTS 2 AND 3, SKYLINE TRACTS; SYCAMORE

Mr. Pierson moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. Brown.  Motion carried.  Voting

“A RESOLUTION REQUIRING THE  MAYOR TO DIRECT PARKS DIVISION TO
RECLAIM A ND R EVEGETATE T HE A REA O F L IONS P ARK D ISTURBED B Y T HE
RECONSTRUCTION O F C ENTRAL AV ENUE.” M r. Segrave r eported t here w as no
recommendation on the item from Finance Committee.  Mr. Laybourn moved to adopt, seconded
by Ms. Case.  D uring c omments a nd u pon inquiry, Todd Feezer, P arks a nd Recreation D ept.
Assistant Director, advised the project is not included in the annual Parks and Recreation Dept.
priority list developed by the department director, and that a funding source has not been identified.
Comments continued and included the following topics: Site as a community gateway; departmental
priorities; estimated cost of $44,200.00 for the project; other “bare” areas in community parks and
along the greenway; suggested use of unexpended department or City Council Ward contingency
funds, and maintenance issues.  Upon inquiry, Barb Dorr, City Treasurer, reviewed the current Parks
and Recreation D ept. f iscal year budget a nd e xpenditures, e xplaining that th e tota l a mount of
expenditures is unknown as the budget year has not ended.  Upon inquiry regarding maintenance of
Parks grounds, Mr. Feezer stated some areas of Lions Park are meant t o be “dry land” areas;
clarified t he site in question is not irrigated, and explained if a funding source is identified t he
revegetation efforts would include soil amendments, seeding and a t emporary irrigation (piping)
system.  Comments were made regarding maintenance of vegetation currently adjacent to the area,
cleanliness of parks, and graffiti and beautification issues.  Motion failed.  Voting “yes” – Ms. Case,
Mr. Laybourn, Dr. Rinne and Mr. Valdez.  Voting “no” – Mr. Brown, Mr. Collins, Mr. Pierson, Mr.
Segrave and Mayor Spiker.

(#5046)

OUTSTANDING CO NSTRUCTION L OAN R ELATING T O THE  REMO DELING A ND

AS A CLINIC TO HOUSE THE YOUTH SERVICES DIVISION AND ADMINISTRATIVE
OFFICES FOR THE LARAMIE COUNTY PEAK WELLNESS CENTER IN CHEYENNE, WY.,
A WYOMING NON-PROFIT ORGANIZATION.”  Barbara Bonds, Freudenthal and Bonds PC
(law firm), advised that appropriate hearings have been held by both the City and County and the
County Commissioners have approved their resolution authorizing bond issuance, and clarified the
City’s resolution is a formality (approval required under IRS Code as both the City and County have
jurisdiction over the site) and that there will be no City legal financial obligation.  Stev e Miller,
resident in the project vicinity, spoke in favor of the project.  Mr. Segrave moved to adopt in an
amount not to exceed $4,150,000.00, seconded by Mr. Valdez.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all
members of the governing body present.  (#5050)

recommendation #1, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Upon inquiry, Brandon Cammarata, Urban Planning
office, expressed his belief that either the Mayor, or his designee, could sign the (final) plat with
Mayor Spiker indicating that Ken Lewis, City Engineer, would be the authorized designee.  Upon
inquiry, Mr. Lewis stated there is no detention pond on the site but there is a drainage channel in the
area.  Concern was expressed with storm drainage flow patterns affecting other area properties.
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.
LEASES/CONTRACTS/LEGAL:
Agreement between the City of Cheyenne, Transit Division, and Stride Learning Center, for
the lease of a transit bus.  Mr. Segrave moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Valdez.  Motion carried.
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present. (#5262)
Agreement between the City of Cheyenne, Transit Division, and Stride Learning Center, to
provide cooperative transit services.  Mr. Segrave moved to approve in an amount not to exceed
$85,554.70, seconded by Mr. Brown.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing
body present. (#5263)
Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and the Cheyenne Animal Shelter, for animal
control services.  Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr. Segrave moved to approve in an amount
not to exceed $248, 766.00, seconded by Mr. Bro wn.  Co mments were m ade o n enforcement
concerns; dogs running at large; Animal Shelter board membership composition; funding assistance;
allowing time for improvement in operations under the new Shelter director, and contracting of
services through the Shelter as a private provider.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of
the governing body present with the exception of Ms. Case, Mr. Laybourn and Mr. Segrave voting
“no”. (#5264)
Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and the Cheyenne Animal Shelter, for animal
shelter services.  Mr. Segrave moved to approve in an amount not to exceed $319,192.00, seconded
by M r. B rown.  C omments w ere m ade re garding c ontractual a rrangements w ith t he S helter,
personnel issues and improvements that have occurred under t he new director. Motion carried.
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present. (#5265)
Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and NextMedia Outdoor, Inc., for the purchase
of advertising space on CTP buses.  Mr. Segrave moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Brown.  Upon
inquiry, Joe Dougherty, Transit Director, advised there is another separate contract with NextMedia
for advertising upon the right-of-way, which includes bus benches, shelters and stops. City Treasurer
Barb Dorr provided information on advertising revenue received for the current fiscal year and Mr.
Dougherty discussed terms of the agreement and revenue amounts.  Mr. Dougherty further advised
that 2 responses to the RFP (Request For Proposal) were received, and provided information on
contract provisions involving the type of advertising that is not allowed. Motion carried.  Voting
“yes” – all members of the governing body present with Mr. Laybourn voting “no”. (#5266)
Contract between the City of Cheyenne and Laramie County for Municipal Court Security
Services.  Mr. Segrave moved to approve in an amount not to exceed $3,000.00, seconded by Ms.
Case.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present. (#5267)
Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Cheyenne Housing Authority, Peak Wellness
Center, Inc., and Peak Wellness Center, Inc., Alcohol Receiving Center, for human services in the
community.  Mr. Segrave moved to approve the agreement with Cheyenne Housing Authority in an
amount not to exceed $47,355.00 and with Peak Wellness Center, Inc., Alcohol Receiving Center
an amount not to exceed $84,955.00, seconded by Mr. Brown. Mr. Segrave reported the agreement
with Peak Wellness Center Inc. was discovered to be a duplication of a 2007 agreement and was,
therefore, being withdrawn from the agenda. Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the
governing body present. (#5268, #5269)
Agreement between the City of Cheyenne, Youth Alternatives and Laramie County, for
juvenile court staffing and pretrial services.  M r. S egrave moved t o approve, seconded by Mr.
Valdez.  Upon inquiry, Ronn Jeffrey, Municipal Court Juvenile Judge, provided information on  the
increase in volume of juvenile cases, and advised he could provide a written report to City Council
regarding the increase.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.
(#5270)
Consideration of  F AA G rant Agreement ( Project # 3-56-0005-39) b etween th e C ity of
Cheyenne, L aramie C ounty, W Y, C heyenne R egional A irport B oard a nd th e F ederal A viation
Administration in an amount not to exceed $7,073,675.00, for rehabilitation of Runway 13/31 at the
Cheyenne Regional Airport -- referred to Finance Committee.  M r. Segrave advised due to the
timeliness of the item to receive funds from the FAA, the item was reviewed at a special meeting of
the Finance Committee and provisions of Title 2 of City Code had been met.  Mr. Segrave moved
to approve, seconded by Mr. Brown.  John Fafoutakis questioned whether the runway would be
concrete or asphalt, and commented on his concerns with asphalt.  Dave Haring, Airport Manager,
clarified the rehabilitation of the runway, which is a secondary runway not the primary runway, will
involve asphalt paving per FAA specifications.  H e advised discussions were held with FAA on
utilizing concrete vs. asphalt and associated costs; provided information on FAA bid construction
specifications and project costs (95% covered by grant), and explained that smaller general aviation
aircraft are the types that utilize Runway 13/31. Upon inquiry regarding compliance with Title 2 of
City Code, City Clerk Carol Intlekofer provided information pursuant to Title 2 involving agenda
items o f t his t ype r equiring review and recommendation by o ne o f t he City Council’s standing
committees prior to final approval.  Following a 5 minute recess,  Mayor Spiker reconvened the
meeting at 7:50 p.m.  Upon request, Ms. Intlekofer read into the record City Code Chapter 2.04,
Section 2.04.160 B. pertaining to referral of agenda items to a City Council standing committee,
stating the types of agenda items not referred to a standing committee include a ppointments,
reappointments and announcements.  M ayor Spiker reviewed t he usual agenda item co mmittee
referral pro cess v s. t he p rocess f ollowed, as  appro ved b y t he C ouncil Pr esident an d F inance
Committee Chair, for this item involving Finance Committee’s review and recommendation of the
item at a special meeting (as publicized) to meet City Code and the FAA time sensitive deadline for
grant acceptance to enable funding for the Airport.  He indicated the County Commissioners and the
Airport Board also held special meetings to provide agreement approval as required.  Upon inquiry,
Mr. Haring explained the unexpected change in the deadline to accept the grant relative to the FAA’s
implementation of a “mini” fiscal year and status of the funding bill in Washington; advised the
rehabilitation project has been on the Airport’s capital improvement program for many years, and
reviewed the allowable time frame for submittal of the grant agreement.  He explained due to FAA
time frames, the request is to approve the entire grant amount of $7,073,675, not just the first grant
agreement amount of $1,073,675; and advised grant funding may potentially be jeopardized if the
agreement is not approved by the time frame (no later than June 24) as the FAA will not commit to
retaining the funding for future grant cycle (application) requests.  Discussion followed and included
further information provided by Mr. Haring on the following topics: Airport’s priority project list,
“boiler plate” language and other provisions contained in FAA grant agreements, grant funding for
airport dev elopment t o reh abilitate t he s econdary run way, v erification t hat t he i tem h ad b een
submitted for the Council’s meeting agenda in a timely manner, which was prior to recent FAA
notification o f t he c hanged d eadline, a nd e ssential u se o f t he r unway a s a  s econdary r unway.
Discussion topics also included runway safety, use and importance of the secondary runway, FAA
grant agreement provisions, and agenda item and standing committee procedures.  Motion carried.
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present with the exception of Mr. Laybourn
voting “no”.  (#5271)
[CA] Contract between the City of Cheyenne, Youth Alternatives, and the State of Wyoming,
Department of Family Services, Division of Juvenile Services for diversion and transitional services
-- referred to Finance Committee.
[CA] Contract b etween th e Ci ty of C heyenne, R ecreation D ivision, a nd J osh C ossitt f or
professional tennis services -- referred to Finance Committee.
[CA] Contract Employment Agreement between the City of Cheyenne, Police Department, and
Tina Henderson for crime victim assistance services -- referred to Finance Committee.
[CA] Contract Employment Agreement between the City of Cheyenne, Police Department, and
Brenda Johnson for crime victim assistance services -- referred to Finance Committee.
[CA] Contract Employment Agreement between the City of Cheyenne, Police Department, and
Carole Anderson-Kincaid for crime victim assistance services -- referred to Finance Committee.
CHANGE ORDERS/CONTRACT MODIFICATIONS:
Contract Modification No. 1 to Contract No. 5244 between the City of Cheyenne and JTL
Group, Inc. d/b/a Knife River for median paving on Missile Drive (2003-2006 1% Sales Tax).  Mr.
Segrave reported there was no recommendation on the item from Finance Committee.  Mr. Collins
moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Pierson.  Discussion followed on information provided and
discussed at Finance Committee; proposed cost (estimated $26,000.00) and scope of the project,
median damage due to train derailment years ago, asphalt vs. concrete median, median uniformity
in appearance, Missile Dr. serving as a community “gateway”, and deteriorating median conditions.
Upon inquiry, Jeff Fanning, Engineering Services Director, provided information concerning work
that would be done under the  modification, stating work would provide median paving around the
Burlington-Northern bridge area, removal of debris and replacement of concrete median paving
around the bridge by filling in gaps between the concrete.  He s tated the majority of the median
damage had occurred when a train derailed at the site several years ago.  Discussion followed on
area aesthetics, 5  penny (1% sales tax) money as the project’s funding source, community entrance,th
recent appr oval an d as pects o f t he M issile Dr.  o verlay pav ing pro ject, an d co ntinuing median
deterioration over time.  Mr. Fanning advised the original scope of the Missile Dr. overlay project
provides for an asphalt overlay on drive lanes and shoulders, and for asphalt curbing in areas where
concrete curbing was deteriorating, and did not include any median paving.  Comments were made
on continuing the original project with the additional modification work, uniformity in appearance,
and materials being utilized. Mr. Fanning clarified the modification involves concrete median paving;
the c urbing will remain asphalt.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – Ms. Case, Mr. Collins, M r.
Laybourn, Mr. Pierson, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Valdez and Mayor Spiker. Voting “no” – Mr. Brown and
Mr. Segrave. (#5244) 
[CA] Contract Modification No. 3 to Contract No. 4607 between the City of Cheyenne, Sanitation
Department, and A-1 Organics to provide resources and services for the public composting facility --
referred to Finance Committee.
[CA] Contract Modification No. 1 to Contract No. 4937 between the City of Cheyenne, Sanitation
Department, an d Cl ean Harb ors f or h azardous was te di sposal services --  ref erred t o F inance
Committee.

 Consideration o f Bid #M-27-08 for a contract between t he City o f Cheyenne and JTL
Group, Inc. d/b/a Knife River, for the fiscal year 2008 intersection repair project (2007-2010 1%
Sales Tax).  Mr. Segrave moved to accept the bid submitted by JTL Group, Inc. d/b/a Knife River,
Cheyenne, in an amount not to exceed $335,159.00, seconded by Mr. Valdez.  Upon inquiry, Jeff
Fanning, Engineering Services Director, advised repairs would be concrete and some small areas of
asphalt patching may be required.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing
body present.  (#5272)
Consideration of Bid #M-32–08 for a contract between the City of Cheyenne and Horton
Rainbow En terprises Co nstruction, f or t he C heyenne Yo uth Activity an d Co mmunity Ce nter
Demolition and Abatement Package Project (Grants and 1% Specific Purpose Sales & Use Tax).
Mr. Segrave moved t o accept t he bid submitted by Horton Rainbow Enterprises Construction,
Cheyenne, in an amount not to exceed $136,280.00, seconded by Mr. Valdez.  Upon inquiry, Todd
Feezer, Parks and Recreation Dept. Assistant Director, advised the work site is the former Kiwanis
Building located at 1317 Parsley and includes asbestos abatement and demolition work (east portion
of the building); explained the scope of work vs. project cost estimates, and clarified only one bidder
submitted a bid.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present with

Consideration of Bid #M-33-08 for a contract between the City o f Cheyenne and S&S

Place (2003-2006 1% Sales Tax).  Mr. Segrave moved to accept the bid submitted by S&S Builders,
LLC, Cheyenne, in an amount not to exceed $393,125.00, seconded by Mr. Brown.  Motion carried.
Voting “yes” - all members of the governing body present.  (#5274)
Consideration of Bid #R-26-08 for one remanufactured lazer tag system from Interspher
International, Inc. for the City of Cheyenne Taco John’s Event Center (2007-2010 1% Sales Tax
& Taco John’s Ice and Events Center funds).  Mr. Segrave moved to accept the bid submitted by
Interspher International, Inc., Des Moines, IA, in an amount not to exceed $57,280.00, seconded
by Mr. Valdez.  U pon inquiry, Delos Searle, Recreation Director, provided information on the
current vs. remanufactured system, warranty provisions and price difference between a n ew vs.
remanufactured system.  Upon inquiry, Barb Dorr, City Treasurer, verified 5th penny (1% sales tax)
money is allocated to the Parks and Recreation Dept., and Mr. Searle provided information on
revenue received from operations at the Events Center.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members
of the governing body present with the exception of Mr. Laybourn voting “no”. 
 
Consideration of Bid #R-34-08 for 4 new medium/heavy duty, body on chassis transit buses
from Intermountain Coach for the City of Cheyenne, Transit Division, which includes the mobile
digital video recorders (FTA Transit Development Grant).  Mr. Segrave moved to accept the bid
submitted by Intermountain Coach, Colorado Springs, CO, in an amount not to exceed $300,760.00,
seconded by Mr. Valdez.  Mo tion carried.  Vo ting “yes” – al l members of the governing body
present.
Consideration of Bid #R-35-08 for furnishing 95 1½ cubic yard steel refuse containers from

submitted b y San i-Pak En vironmental, No rfolk, NE,  in an amount n ot t o ex ceed $31, 181.85,
seconded by Mr. Brown.  Motion carried.  Vo ting “yes” – al l members of the governing body

Corp., for the Norris Viaduct Replacement Project (1% Specific Purpose Sales & Use Tax and

APPOINTMENTS:
Appointments of Larry Atwell and Janet Cowley to the Cheyenne-Laramie County Economic
Development Joint Powers Board, Marty Crowe, to t he Contractor Licensing Board, and Marty
Griffith to the Greenway Advisory Committee.  Mr. Collins moved to approve, seconded by Mr.
Segrave.  Mayor Spiker stated as the appointments and reappointments (next agenda item) are
Mayoral appointments, he would not be voting.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the
governing body present with the exception of Mayor Spiker who did not vote.
Reappointments of Jo Butler, Jay Guerin, Dick Krahenbuhl, Bebe Molway, Bradley Oberg,
Carol Matteson-Pascal, Liberty Blain, Doyle Travis, Tom DeHoff, and Abby Yenco to the Greenway
Advisory Committee.  Mr. Collins moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Pierson.  Motion carried.
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present with the exception of Mayor Spiker who
did not vote (Mayoral appointments).
ANNOUNCEMENTS/REPORTS/MOTIONS:
Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held July 14, 2008 at 6:00 p.m. in City
Council Chambers to determine whether compliance of annexation conditions required by Wyo. Stat.
§15-1-402 exists for the property described as the W½ of Tract 10, Airport Valley Tracts, Laramie
County, WY (aka 5905 Crow Road).
OTHER CITY BUSINESS:
John Fafoutakis expressed concerns with graffiti “tagging” and encouraged efforts by law
enforcement to curtail gang activity in the community.
Mayor Spiker announced City employee Norm Soden would be retiring after 25 years of
service and expressed appreciation to Mr. Soden for his commitment to the City. 
There being no further business to come before the governing body, the meeting was
officially adjourned at 8:40 p.m.
 Submitted by,
 
 Carol A. Intlekofer, CMC 
 City Clerk

MEETING: Cheyenne City Council | DATE: 2008-07-14 | CLIP_ID: 34 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 34

 RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
July 14, 2008
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date at 6:00 p.m.
in Council Chambers.  Present were: MAYOR-Jack R. Spiker; COUNCIL MEMBERS-Mr. Brown,
Ms. Broyles, Ms. Case, Mr. Collins, Mr. Laybourn, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Segrave and Mr. Valdez.  Absent:
Mr. Pierson.
 
Also present: Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk; Barb Dorr, City Treasurer, and Claudia Angelos,
City Attorney.
Consent Agenda. (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to be
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests, in which event the item
will be removed from the Consent Agenda and considered in its normal sequence on the agenda.)
Upon request by Mr. Laybourn, Mayor Spiker announced agenda items #12, 1 9(a)(b)(c)(d)(e),
20(a)(b) and 21(b) would be removed from the Consent Agenda. Mr. Collins moved to approve
remaining Consent Agenda items, seconded by Mr. Brown.  M otion carried.  Voting “yes” – all
members of the governing body present with the exception of Mr. Laybourn voting “no”.
[CA] MINUTES FROM REGULAR MEETING OF THE GOVERNING BODY ON JUNE 23,
2008.  Motion (per consent agenda) carried.
[CA] MINUTES FROM VOUCHERS MEETING OF THE GOVERNING BODY ON JULY 7,
2008.  Motion (per consent agenda) carried.
Mr. Segrave  moved to approve payment of vouchers as presented, seconded by Mr. Valdez.
Upon inquiry, City Treasurer Barb Dorr reviewed information available on vouchers payable to:
Desert Mountain Corp. ($18,276.59), road saver material (a/k/a ice slicer); Janisan ($934.00), smoker
stations (6)  f or Golf Di vision; K nife R iver ( $117,147.07), co ncrete wo rk, A DA co rners, al ley
approach and granite, and Adam Martin ($200.00), Plaza event security.  Discussion followed on the
project work area involving services by Knife River; Plaza event security services; voucher review
procedures, and availability of staff to provide information on voucher expenditures.  Main motion
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present with the exception of Ms. Case
and Mr. Laybourn voting “no”.  Upon inquiry under other City business, Mike Abel, Public Works
Director, stated he could obtain and provide information to Council members on whether the voucher
payment to Knife River included services for work conducted on Thomes Ave.
Mayor Spiker opened a public hearing for the purpose of receiving comments and input to
determine whether compliance of annexation conditions required by Wyo. Stat. §15-1-402 exists for
the property described as the W½ of Tract 10, Airport Valley Tracts, Laramie County, WY (aka 5905
Crow Road).  Brandon Cammarata, Urban Planning office, reviewed requirements and compliance
of conditions contained in Wyo. Stat. §15-1-402, including specific information indicating compliance
with Wyo. Stat. §15-1-402(a)(i) through (vi). There being no further comments or input and purpose
of the hearing having been met, Mayor Spiker closed the hearing.

Department, and A-1 Organics to provide resources and services for the public composting facility.
Mr. Segrave moved to approve in an amount not to exceed $189,312.80, seconded by Mr. Valdez.
Upon inquiry, Dennis Pino, Solid Waste Director, provided information on monitoring of compost
contents to meet nationally recognized standards.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of
the governing body present.  (#4607)
Contract Modification No. 1 to Contract No. 4937 between the City of Cheyenne, Sanitation
Department, and Clean Harbors for hazardous waste disposal services.  Mr. S egrave moved to
approve in an amount not to exceed $200,000.00, seconded by Mr. Brown. Upon inquiry, Dennis
Pino, Solid Waste Director, clarified hazardous waste includes such items as pesticides, paints, oils,
cleaners and solvents, etc., but does not include electronic waste.  He stated the department works
closely w ith Laramie County a s th ey have funding for a  p rogram th at a ssists w ith d isposing of
hazardous waste products.  M otion carried.  Vo ting “yes” – al l members of the governing body
present.  (#4937)
[CA] Contract Modification No. 1 to Contract No. 5108 between the City of Cheyenne, Parks &
Recreation Dept ., and Sco tch Bo y Landscaping, for irrigation and sod in Western Hills Park –
referred to Finance Committee.
[CA] Contract M odification No . 2 t o Co ntract No . 4968 b etween t he C ity o f Ch eyenne,
Engineering Dept., and the Wyoming Dept. of Transportation, to add a supplemental project review
meeting and a public scoping meeting for reconstruction of W. Pershing Blvd. from I-25 to Pioneer
Ave. (2003-2006 1% Sales Tax) – referred to Finance Committee.
CONSIDERATION OF BIDS:
Consideration of Bid #M-22-08 for a contract between the City of Cheyenne and Reiman
Corp., for t he Norris Viaduct Replacement Project (1% Specific Purpose Sales & U se Tax and
Federal Grant funds).  John Rogers, representing Scott Contracting, Inc., Henderson, CO, a bidder
on the project, expressed concerns regarding recommendation for approval relative to selection of
the lowest base bidder and staff proposal involving alternates. He provided a brief summary of bid
differences as submitted by Scott Contracting and Reiman Corporation; discussed a 2002 Wyoming
Supreme Court case; provided copies of a worksheet comparing bids between the two companies,
with alternates, and explained Scott Contracting’s reason for submitting an alternate concrete staining
material other than what was specified in the bid document.  He stated that although Reiman may
have submitted the lowest base bid, Scott Contracting had the lowest overall bid when including the
alternates.  Barton Puryear, in-house staff counsel for Scott Contracting, advised their company’s bid
would provide savings to the City; reviewed bid criteria; indicated that several supplies and manpower
would be obtained locally, and encouraged the bid be awarded to Scott Contracting.  John Fafoutakis
spoke in favor of Scott Contracting, Inc.’s bid due to overall cost savings and use of taxpayers’
dollars.  Gwendolyn Connell and Debbie Brennan spoke in favor of awarding the bid to a Wyoming
company that will assist with the local economy.  Mr. Rogers addressed the local contractor bid vs.
out-of-state bid and explained as there is a substantial amount of Federal funding involved, the bid
was conducted without consideration of the in-state bidder process.  He referenced recent projects
(Laramie County Library and St. Mary’s School) involving out-of-state companies that have hired
subcontractors locally and purchased supplies locally, as Scott Contracting would do.  Mr. Segrave
reported there was no recommendation on the item from Finance Committee. Ms. Broyles moved to
accept the base bid from Reiman Corp., Cheyenne, with Alternatives #1, 3, 4, 5(b) and 6 in an amount
not to exceed $18,389,734.60, seconded by Mr. Valdez.  Discussion included the following topics:
Project as a high priority for the community; aesthetics and amenities involved with some of the
proposed alternates; available funding and assistance from grants and 1% Specific Purpose sales tax;
monetary assistance from Frontier Refining for Alternate #6; project eligibility for State Loan and
Investment funding, and public meetings and input on  the project a nd alternates desired.  U pon
inquiry, Barb Dorr, City Treasurer, provided information on 1% Specific Purpose sales tax collection
amounts and current fund balance, grant monies received and estimated project funding shortage, and
City Engineer Ken Lewis clarified funding for Alternate #6 would be provided by Frontier Refining
in t he amount o f $1.5 million do llars. Discussion co ntinued o n awarding t he bid with alternates
without having all of the funding in place, review of the proposed alternates and options for those
involving aesthetics, building a basic efficient bridge structure, manpower to maintain landscaped
areas, and bid document language and the bidding process.  Upon inquiry, Mark Stark, Olsson and
Associates, design team project manager, confirmed he had toured some past concrete projects in the
community during the initial stages of the Norris Bridge project, and provided information on fees
paid to Olsson and Associates for its project services.  During comments concerning the quality of
concrete i nvolved i n p rojects th roughout th e c ommunity, c oncrete s tandards, a nd l ife s pan of
concrete, Mr. Brown raised a point of order questioning the relevancy of comments being made.
Mayor Spiker ruled comments could continue.  Upon inquiry, Rich Bolkovatz, chairman of the City’s
past co mmittee t hat assisted with review and modification o f t he City’s co ncrete specifications,
advised m odification reco mmendations t o co ncrete s pecifications were agreed upo n b y l ocal
contractors serving on the committee. Discussion continued on City concrete specifications, condition
of failing concrete and projected life span; review of bid alternates and citizens’ input in determining
alternate proposals; bid document wording; awarding of bid based upon lowest base bid received;
fiduciary respo nsibility t o t axpayers; l ocal an d n on-local b idders o n pro jects; en gineers pro ject
estimate; staff proposed alternates; importance of alternates involving traffic signalization and storm
sewer w ork; a lternates involving lighting a nd c oncrete staining; c ommunity beautification, p ark
environment and aesthetics; voter support for the project; quality infrastructure, and project costs.
Upon inquiry, City At torney Claudia Angelos, a dvised t he bid d ocument c ould be followed by
awarding the bid based upon the base amount and adding alternates to it. Motion failed. Voting “yes”
– Ms. Broyles and Mr. Valdez.  Voting “no” – Mr. Brown, Ms. Case, Mr. Collins, Mr. Laybourn,
Dr. Rinne, Mr. Segrave and Mayor Spiker.  Mr. Valdez moved to approve the base bid submitted by
Reiman Corp. in the amount of $16,858,928.17 with alternates #4 and #6 for a total bid amount of
$17,168,638.00 a nd re quest s taff e nter i nto v alue e ngineering d iscussions with t he c ontractor,
seconded by Ms. Broyles. Following a ten minute recess, Mayor Spiker reconvened the meeting at
8:00 p.m.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Lewis clarified the total bid amount is $17,168,638.00 including the two
alternates (#4 and #6). Mr. Fafoutakis commented o n one o f t he alternates (not included in the
motion) t hat would provide for a park facility in relationship t o its location, building roads and
structures that will last, and using fiduciary judgment to award the bid to the lowest bidder. Upon
query, Mr. Lewis advised that by adding alternates #4 and #6 to the lowest bid submitted, Reiman’s
bid would be the lowest by approximately $103,000.00, and explained that “value engineering” means
the contractor will look at more efficient means and less expensive materials to approach the same
project to receive an equal and acceptable end product.  He verified if, as a result of value engineering
discussions, any contract modifications were determined to be presented they would come before the
governing body for approval.  Further discussion followed on project costs, funding, scenario of
adding remaining alternates in the future, and engineering estimate affiliated with Alternate #6.  In
conjunction with the estimated time frame in which results of value engineering discussions could be
provided, M r. L ewis indicated information c ould p ossibly be p rovided w ithin a pproximately 3
months, an d adv ised ce rtain addi tional alternates t hat m ay b e co nsidered as  a res ult o f v alue
engineering discussions would have to be issued to the same contractor. Motion carried.  Voting
“yes” – all members of the governing body present with the exception of Ms. Case and Mr. Laybourn
voting “no”. (#5280)
Consideration o f Bid # M-34-08 for a  c ontract between t he City o f Cheyenne a nd STC
Construction Co., Inc., for the FY 2008 M illing and Utility Adjustments Project (2003-2006 1%
Sales Ta x). M r. S egrave m oved to a ccept t he b id s ubmitted b y STC  C onstruction C o., In c.,
Cheyenne, i n a n a mount n ot t o e xceed $ 134,925.00, s econded by M r. B rown.  U pon i nquiry
concerning whether any roads to b e rehabilitated were located within Ward II, Ken Lewis, City
Engineer, advised he could provide a list of project areas.  Discussion followed on road project work
and needs in the City’s three different wards.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the
governing body present with the exception of Dr. Rinne and Mr. Segrave voting “no”. (#5281)
[CA] Consideration of  B id # R-1-09 f or f urnishing tw o n ew tw in s crew c ab &  chassis f rom
Cheyenne Freightliner, for the Sanitation Dept. – referred to Finance Committee. 
[CA] Consideration of Bid #R-2-09 for furnishing four solid waste transfer trailers from Ameri-
Tech Equipment, for the Sanitation Dept. – referred to Finance Committee.
ANNOUNCEMENTS/REPORTS/MOTIONS:
Announcement was made that a Calcutta will be held July 26, 2008 beginning at 5:30 p.m.
at the Cheyenne Frontier Days VIP/Sponsor Tent located in Frontier Park in conjunction with the
2008 Cheyenne Frontier Days Rodeo.  Proceeds to benefit Cheyenne area charities.
Announcement was made of the appointment of Richard Keslar to the Tourism Promotion
Joint Powers Board, and Nancy Abraham to the Historic Preservation Board, to be confirmed July
28, 2008.
Announcement was made of the reappointment of Connie Sloan-Cathcart to the Public Transit
Advisory Board, to be confirmed July 28, 2008.
OTHER CITY BUSINESS:
John Fafoutakis spoke generally on project cost over-runs, expressing his hope that this will
not b e an  i ssue wi th t he No rris Vi aduct Re placement Pr oject, an d i n f avor o f b uilding b asic,
dependable construction projects and the need to repair roads.
Upon inquiry regarding whether residents are allowed access to fish at Lake Absarraca during
Frontier Days, Rick Parish, Parks and Recreation Director, clarified persons wishing to fish during
the Frontier Days event can have access and just need to identify their purpose with CFD security
individuals at the Lake entrance.
There being no further business to come before the governing body, the meeting was officially
adjourned at 8:33 p.m.
 Submitted by,
 
 Carol A. Intlekofer, CMC 
 City Clerk
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request.

 August 2, 2008

Office of City Clerk
July 28, 2008
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date at 6:00 p.m.
in Council Chambers.  Present were: MAYOR-Jack R. Spiker; COUNCIL MEMBERS-Mr. Brown,
Ms. Broyles, Ms. Case, Mr. Collins (arriving at 6:04 p.m.), Mr. Laybourn, Mr. Pierson, Dr. Rinne,
Mr. Segrave and Mr. Valdez.
 
Also present: Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk; Barb Dorr, City Treasurer, and Erik Rasmussen,
Deputy City Attorney.
Consent Agenda. (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to be
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests, in which event the item
will be removed from the Consent Agenda and considered in its normal sequence on the agenda.)
Upon request by Mr. Laybourn, Mayor Spiker announced agenda items #15, 16, 23(a)(b), 24(a)(b),
and 26(a)(b) would be removed from the Consent Agenda. Comments were made pertaining to
removal of Consent Agenda items.  Mr.  Brown moved to approve remaining Consent Agenda items,
seconded by Mr. Pierson.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present
with the exception of Mr. Laybourn and Mr. Segrave voting “no”.

2008.  Motion (per consent agenda) carried.

2008.  Motion (per consent agenda) carried.
Mr. Segrave  moved to approve payment of vouchers as presented, seconded by Mr. Valdez.

Appraisals, I nc. ( $3,150.00), p roperty a ppraisal f or 4 500 D ell R ange B lvd. f or P arks D ivision
(appraised value approximately $1.1 million).  Mr. Collins (upon arrival) declared a conflict of interest
(business interest) due to a voucher payable to the Bicycle Station.  Upon further inquiry, Ms. Dorr
reviewed i nformation a vailable f or v ouchers p ayable to:  F rederika B arela ( $36.73), Y outh
Alternatives employee, reimbursement for ink cartridges; Gade Concrete ($1,100.00), excavate, form
and pour sidewalk to restroom at Pointe Park, and Soothing Sensations ($105.00), refund of business
license application fees.  Main motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body
with the exception of Ms. Case and Mr. Laybourn voting “no”, and Mr. Collins who did not vote.
“AN ORDINANCE AMENDING SECTION 2.04.160(D)(6), READINGS-COMMITTEE
REPORTS-AMENDMENTS, OF CHAPTER 2.04, MAYOR AND CITY COUNCIL, OF TITLE
2, ADMINISTRATION AND PERSONNEL, OF THE CODE OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE,
WYOMING.”  Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr. Segrave moved to approve on second reading,
seconded by Mr. Brown. Upon inquiry as to the purpose of the ordinance, Mayor Spiker advised the
state legislature at its last session increased the amount required for advertising of contract bids or
response from $20,000.00 t o $35,000.00 and t he o rdinance is in co njunction with t he state law
change.  Upon inquiry, City Treasurer Barb Dorr stated the change factors in inflation and increased
costs for public improvement projects; provided information on oversight of those projects involving
costs up to the $35,000.00 threshold, including governing body approval of vouchers, and indicated
the last ordinance involving this topic ($20, 000.00 threshold) was approved in 1995.  Discussion
followed on  p urchasing r equirements, p rocedures involving c alls f or b ids a nd/or r equests f or
proposals/quotes, governing body involvement and oversight in purchasing process, City Council
departmental bu dget a pproval a uthority, a nd C ity Attorney staff w ritten o pinion indicating t he
ordinance sponsor did not have a conflict of interest in sponsoring the ordinance.  Upon inquiry
regarding whether other cities have implemented similar ordinance changes, Mayor Spiker advised
it was unknown if other cities/towns had or were enacting similar changes, and stated the Wyoming
Association of Municipalities (WAM), whose members consist of all 99 communities within the state,
supported and lobbied for the change during the legislative session.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes”
– all members of the governing body with the exception of Mr. Laybourn and Dr. Rinne voting “no”.

LEASES/CONTRACTS/LEGAL:
Contract between the City of Cheyenne, Police Department, and Laramie County School
District No. 1, for School Resource Officers for the 2008-2009 school.  M r. Segrave moved to
approve in an amount not to exceed $329,606.00, seconded by Mr. Valdez.  Motion carried.  Voting
“yes” – all members of the governing body.  (#5285)
Agreement between the City of Cheyenne, Frontier Mall Associates LP, and CBL Peripheral
Properties LP, for Frontier Mall Drainage, addressing p roperty det ention requirements t hrough
participation in construction of a regional detention basin.  Mr. Segrave moved to approve, seconded
by Mr. Brown.  Upon inquiry, City Engineer Ken Lewis explained the agreement will allow Frontier
Mall Properties to participate in the regional detention basin that will be created by the Dry Creek
FEMA p roject; in dicated t he ma ll p roperties fin ancial p articipation w ill b e a pproximately
$116,000.00, and advised any opportunity for future properties to participate in the detention basin
would be reviewed by staff and FEMA and would include individual review of the property relative
to drainage calculations, impact, and related issues.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of
the governing body.  (#5286)
[CA] Maintenance Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and the Wyoming Department of
Transportation f or t he i nstallation an d m aintenance o f en hancements f or t he R andall A venue
Interchange Project  (2003-2006 1% Sales Tax) -- referred to Finance Committee.
CHANGE ORDERS/CONTRACT MODIFICATIONS:
Contract Modification No. 1 to Contract No. 5108 between the City of Cheyenne, Parks &
Recreation Dept., and Scotch Boy Landscaping, for irrigation and sod in Western Hills Park.  Mr.
Segrave moved to approve in an amount not to exceed $7,400.00, seconded by Mr. Brown.  Motion
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.  (#5108)
Contract M odification No . 2 t o Co ntract No . 4968 b etween t he C ity o f Ch eyenne,
Engineering Dept., and the Wyoming Dept. of Transportation, to add a supplemental project review
meeting and a public scoping meeting for reconstruction of W. Pershing Blvd. from I-25 to Pioneer
Ave. (2003-2006 1% Sales Tax).  Mr. Segrave moved to approve in an amount no t t o exceed
$10,440.00, seconded by Mr. Valdez. Upon inquiry, City Engineer Ken Lewis advised the Wyoming
Dept. of Transportation (WYDOT) has already held these meetings but additional meetings will be
held prior to final approval of t he project, and that this is a WYDOT assisted project following
WYDOT’s schedule.  He explained the amount of the contract modification includes individual staff
consultants’ hours to compile data, prepare documents and visual aids, attend meetings, and any post-
meeting and peripheral activities.  Discussion included timeliness of submission of the modification
relative to WYDOT’s schedule and the meetings already being held; City and WYDOT contract
management issues; project information and meeting notification to affected neighborhood residents;
federal funding involved in the project; project including work at the Randall Ave. interchange of I-
25, and support for keeping affected residents informed.  Mr. Lewis provided information concerning
WYDOT’s public meetings and residents’ notification efforts, and stated there are tw o p rojects
involved, WYDOT’s Randall Interchange Reconstruction project, and t he City’s Pershing West
Reconstruction project that WYDOT is doing on the City’s behalf.  He confirmed the State is the lead
agency for the contracts.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body  with
the exception of Mr. Laybourn voting “no”.  (#4968)
[CA] Amendment No. 3 to Contract No. 3516 between the City of Cheyenne and the Wyoming
Association o f Risk Management P roperty I nsurance Joint P owers Board for a n Amended a nd
Restated Joint Powers Agreement -- referred to Finance Committee.

Consideration of  B id # R-1-09 f or f urnishing two  new tw in s crew c ab &  c hassis f rom
Cheyenne Freightliner, for the Sanitation Dept.  Mr. Segrave moved to accept the bid submitted by
Cheyenne Freightliner in an amount not to e xceed $203,204.00, seconded by Mr. Valdez. Upon
inquiry, Vicki Nemecek, Public Works Dept., confirmed her attendance at a recent Council work
session involving discussion on the anticipated life span of the current landfill and related future
landfill options.  Comment followed regarding whether documentation was available on the potential
life span a nd c apacity of the e xisting landfill and how t ransportation o f waste t o Colorado w ill
increase the life span time frame. Mr. Segrave raised  a point of order indicating that comments being
made were not relevant to the motion on the floor as the decision to transfer solid waste to Ault, CO
had already been decided at a previous meeting.  Mayor Spiker ruled in favor of the point of order,
indicating comments were “off subject”, and requested discussion remain relevant to the motion to
act on the bid to purchase two vehicles for use in transporting waste to Colorado.  During comments
and upon inquiry, Ms. Nemecek provided information on sanitation’s equipment replacement plan
and discussed factors involving fuel costs and the number of trips that may be made to the Ault site
based o n c ommunity waste v olume and r ecycling e fforts.  M otion c arried.  V oting “ yes” –  a ll
members of the governing body with the exception of Ms. Case and Mr. Laybourn voting “no”.
Consideration of Bid #R-2-09 for furnishing four solid waste transfer trailers from Ameri-
Tech Equipment, for the Sanitation Dept.  Mr. Segrave moved to accept the bid submitted by Ameri-
Tech Equipment, Casper, in an amount not to exceed $287,600.00, seconded by Mr. Brown.  Motion
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the exception of Ms. Case and Mr.
Laybourn voting “no”.
[CA] Consideration of Bid #R-4-09 for furnishing 646 1½ cubic yard refuse containers from Ameri-
Tech Equipment Co., Casper WY, for the Sanitation Dept. -- referred to Finance Committee.
APPOINTMENTS:
Appointment of Richard Keslar to the Tourism Promotion Joint Powers Board, and Nancy
Abraham t o t he Historic Preservation Board.  M r. Collins moved t o approve, seconded by Mr.
Segrave.  Motion carried. Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.
Reappointment of Connie Sloan-Cathcart to the Public Transit Advisory Board.  Mr. Segrave
moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Collins. Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the
governing body.
ANNOUNCEMENTS/REPORTS/MOTIONS:
[CA] Annual leaseholder improvement report submitted by the Airport Golf Club, Inc. -- referred
to Finance Committee.
Announcement was made of the appointment of  Ami Skeens to the Public Transit Advisory
Board, and Peter Illoway to the Cheyenne Regional Airport Board, to be confirmed August 11, 2008.
Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held August 11, 2008 at 6:00 p.m. in City
Council Chambers to determine whether compliance of annexation conditions required by Wyo. Stat.
§ 15-1-402 exists for the property described as being situated in a portion of Section 1, 12, and 17,
T13N., R67W. of the 6  P.M., Laramie County, WY (located S. of I-80 and predominantly E. ofth
Southwest Dr.).
OTHER CITY BUSINESS:
Resident John Fafoutakis encouraged placement of a plastics recycling drop-off bin in the
south Cheyenne area, and expressed concerns with individuals on ATVs he has observed harassing
an antelope herd in a construction area near Walterscheid.
Mayor Spiker recognized former City Councilman Tom Scherr, who was present at t he
meeting.
Upon i nquiry regardi ng pro posed ac quisition of pro perty l ocated at  4500 Del l Ra nge
Blvd.(agenda item #22), Mayor Spiker and Rick Parish, Parks and Recreation Director, verified an
offer t o purch ase h as b een made an d i s co ntingent upo n go verning b ody appro val; pro vided
information on due diligence that has been conducted (i.e. building inspections, etc.); funding that has
been approved to put toward a new facility, and stated the site will provide for relocation of the Parks
Maintenance Shop due to construction of the new Children’s Garden near the Botanic Gardens.
Comment was made regarding the property transaction process and communication of information
to City Council members.
Concern was expressed regarding street, curb and gutter debris along the Cheyenne Frontier
Days parade route and the downtown area during the recent celebration in conjunction with the Clean
and Sweep program.  It was noted that the increase in volume of persons in Cheyenne during CFD,
and the number of events in the downtown area, make it a challenge to keep up with clean-up efforts.
All City employees involved with the CFD event were commended for their efforts during this year’s
celebration.
There being no further business to come before the governing body, the meeting was officially
adjourned at 7:13 p.m.
 Submitted by,
 
 Carol A. Intlekofer, MMC
 City Clerk
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request.
Published: Wyoming Tribune-Eagle
 August 5, 2008
