MEETING: Cheyenne City Council | DATE: 2008-08-11 | CLIP_ID: 39 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 39

   RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
August 11, 2008
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date at 6:00 p.m.
in Council Chambers.  Present were: MAYOR-Jack R. Spiker; COUNCIL MEMBERS-Mr. Brown,
Ms. Broyles, Ms. Case, Mr. Collins, Mr. Laybourn, Mr. Pierson, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Segrave and Mr.
Valdez.  The pledge of allegiance was recited.
 
Also present: Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk; Barb Dorr, City Treasurer, and Claudia Angelos,
City Attorney.
Consent Agenda. (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA]  are considered to be
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests, in which event the
item will be removed f rom the Consent Agenda and considered in its normal sequence  on the
agenda.)  Upon request by Mr. Laybourn, Mayor Spiker announced agenda items #21, 23, 24, 34(a),
35(a), 36(a), 37(a) and 38(a) would be removed from the Consent Agenda.   Comment was made
pertaining to removal of Consent Agenda items. Mr. Collins moved to approve remaining Consent
Agenda item s, seconded by Mr. Pierson.  Motion carr ied.  Voting  “yes” – a ll members of the
governing body with the exception of Mr. Laybourn voting “no”.
[CA] MINUTES FROM REGULAR MEETING OF THE GOVERNING BODY ON JULY 28,
2008.  Motion (per consent agenda) carried.
[CA] MINUTES FROM VOUCHERS MEETING OF THE GOVERNING BODY ON AUGUST
4, 2008.  Motion (per consent agenda) carried.
Mr. Segrave  moved to approve payment of vouchers as presented, seconded by Mr. Brown.
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. 
Mayor Spiker opened a public hearing for the purpose of receiving comments and input to
determine whether compliance of annexation conditions required by Wyo. Stat. § 15-1-402 exists
for the property described as being situated in a portion of Section 1, 12, and 17, T.13N., R.67W.
of the 6  P.M., Laramie County, WY (located S. of I-80 and predominantly E. of Southwest Dr.).th
Brandon Cammarata, Urban Planning office, reviewed requirements and compliance of conditions
contained in Wyo. Stat. §15-1-402, including specific information indicating compliance with Wyo.
Stat. §15-1-402(a)(i) through (vi).  The following individuals expressed concerns with the proposed
annexation:  L eo Garcia, J ohn Fafoutakis, Glenn Connor, F rancis Shi ve, Ruby Tomlinson and
Brenda Rath.  Topics discussed included: Public safety and train/vehicular volume concerns with
the railroad crossing at Southwest Drive off West Lincolnway; area traffic congestion and delays,
including effect on emergency responders; problems encountered with alternate traffic routes due
to impassable times at the crossing; suggestion of installing an overpass or underpass (tunnel) at the
crossing; verbal and written safety and traffic concerns received from railroad representatives and
former City staff; statistics on train volumes and injuries/deaths occurring at railroad crossings;
proposed housing development plans for the property and effect on residents in the vicinity relative
to traffic and safety; uses of property in the area (i.e. petroleum storage, pipeline); traffic study;
increased traffic due to o ther nearby developments, and drainage concerns.  Du ring comments,
various speakers submitted written information (on file with office of City Clerk) to governing body
members in support of their statements.  Gay Woodhouse, attorney representing landowners in the
surrounding area of the proposed property to be annexed, submitted a written petition of protest
signed by landowners, and spoke in opposition to annexation of the property.  She reviewed state
statute annexation provisions and requir ements outlined in Wy o. Stat. §15-1-402, and discusse d
traffic in the ar ea, including the e ffect of semi-tr uck “backups” from I -25 affecting College Dr.
traffic; health, safety and we lfare concerns; creation of a ne w county pocket if a nnexation were
approved; water  and sew er se rvices; property  contig uity; exi sting ne ighborhood lifesty le, and
potential future financial effect of annexation on nearby residents relative to repair/replacement of
septic systems. Upon inquiry regarding property use if the proposed housing development did not
occur, Ken Clark, Garrett Properties, LLC, one of the landowners of the property proposed to be
annexed, responded that he has been approached by other interested parties inquiring about use of
the property for other potential projec ts other than a housing  development.  Upon inquiry , Mr.
Cammarata revie wed the c urrent location of the Chey enne A nimal Shelter (CAS) fa cility and
confirmed the entity was a participant in the request for annexation, and provided property zoning
classification information for the proposed annexation site.  Pursuant to inquiry concerning the new
CAS site/building, City Treasurer Barb Dorr stated the City does not hold the mortgage for the CAS
property.  There being no further comments or input and purpose of the hearing having been met,
Mayor Spiker closed the hearing.
“AN ORDINANCE AMENDING SECTION 2.04.160(D)(6), READINGS-COMMITTEE
REPORTS-AMENDMENTS, OF CHAPTER 2.04, MAYOR AND CITY COUNCIL, OF TITLE
2, ADMINISTRATION AND PERSONNEL, OF THE CODE OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE,
WYOMING.”  Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr. Segrave moved to approve on third and final
reading, seconde d by  Mr. Valde z.  C omments included the following  topics: Monitoring of
expenditures; accountability and fiscal responsibility; City Council oversight in reviewing purchases;
effects of inf lation on project c osts and purcha ses; ordi nance sponsor rela tive to the ordinanc e
subject; City Council departmenta l budget approval; 2008 state leg islative action increasing the
threshold amount (from $20,000.00 to $35,000.00) required for advertising of bids, and Wyoming
Association of Municipalities’ support for the state change.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all
members of the governing body with the exception of Ms. Case, Mr. Laybourn and Dr. Rinne voting
“no”. (#3818)
“AN ORDINANCE ANNEXING TO THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, PARCELS
OF LAND DESCRIBED AS A TRACT OF LAND SITUATED IN A PORTION OF THE NW¼
OF SECTION 8, AND A PORTION OF WALTERSCHEID BOULEVARD SITUATED IN A
PORTION OF THE NE¼  OF SECTION 7 AND A PORTION OF THE NW¼ OF SECTION 8,
T.13N., R .66W. OF THE 6  P.M. AND A PORTION OF WEST JEFFERSON ROAD LYINGTH
BETWEEN THE WESTERLY RIGHT-OF-WAY LINE OF THE SOUTH GREELEY HIGHWAY
AND T HE WE ST L INE O F T RACT 1 6 A LLISON T RACTS, L ARAMIE CO UNTY, WY
(LOCATED S. OF AND ADJACENT TO FOX FARM RD. AND E. OF AND ADJACENT TO
WALTERSCHEID B LVD.).”  R.C. R eiman, with Gate way Sou th, reque sted the ordinanc e be
continued for 30 days.  Reporting for Public Services Committee, Mr. Collins moved to approve on
third and final reading, seconded by Dr. Rinne.  Mr. Collins moved to postpone for 30 days (to Sept.
8, 2008), seconded by Mr. Brown.  Mr. Reiman advised a postponement would allow for further
discussion on preliminary plat issues with City staff. Upon inquiry, Martin Matsen, Development
Director, advised there are engineering issues they are attempting to resolve on the preliminary plat
and suggested the plat could be approved concurrently with annexation action or after the ordinance
was approved.  Mr. Matsen provided information on the area involving engineering and right-of-way
issues and effect of topography in the area, and Mr. Reiman spoke on right -of-way concerns and
current dirt (fill) work.  Mr. Reiman clarified he had written documentation from the South Side
Water and Sewer District office stating the District will provide services to the property.  Comments
followed on South S ide Water a nd Sewer District’s policies involving a cceptance or denia l of
properties to receive services.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Matsen stated the postponement will allow staff
more time to work with the developer.  Motion to postpone for 30 days carried.  Voting “yes” –  all
members of the governing body.
Following a 10 minute recess, Mayor Spiker reconvened the meeting at 7:30 p.m.
“AN ORDINANCE AMENDING TITLE 5, BUSINESS LICENSES AND REGULATIONS,
CHAPTER 5.12, ALCOHOLIC BEVERAGES, SECTION 5.12.090 (B), MINORS-DISPENSING
ROOM RESTRI CTIONS, OF T HE CODE OF  THE CI TY OF  CHEYENNE, WYOMI NG,
CONCERNING MI NORS E MPLOYED I N A  RES TAURANT AND F ULL SER VICE
RESTAURANT ENVIRONMENT.”  The following individuals, affiliated with City retail and bar
and grill type liquor licenses, spoke in favor of the ordinance:  Mylon Skala, Applebees; Rachael
Svobode and John Johnson, Old Chicago; Thomas Rudolph and James Yates, Sanfords Grub and
Pub, and Jay Hutchinson, Olive Garden.  Topics discussed by the speakers included: Management,
workforce/staffing levels and operational issues regarding employees in their respective businesses,
which involve the sale of food as the primary purpose of business, who are 18, 19 and 20 years of
age and their inability, under City Code, to enter the dispensing room to serve  as host/hostesses
(seating guests) or to deliver  food/drinks to dining pa trons; current state statutes and City  Code,
which allows those employees who are at least 18 years of age (in a restaurant environment) to serve
alcohol to all other dining areas of the restaurant, except the dispensing room, through walk-up area
access; clarification that t he definition of “serving ” does not include  mixing and pouring drinks
(bartending); importance of being able to utilize persons ages 18-21 as a labor source, and labor pool
shortage issues affecting customer service levels. Lynn Birleffi, Wyoming Lodging and Restaurant
Assoc., spoke in support of the ordinance, stating this is an important issue to many local restaurant
businesses.  She discussed workforce/labor shortage problems; that employees entering a dispensing
room would do so only during employment hours for work purposes; that employees must follow
liquor licensees’ employment policies to ensure continued employment, and that other communities
allow this in a variety  of diffe rent spectrums since it is al lowed by state statutes.  Ms. Birlef fi
submitted written “Talking Points” in support of her statements (on file with office of City Clerk).
Alfreda Gonzales, Exec. Dir., Laramie County Community Partnership, advised the Partnership is
working on the issue of underage drinking and that results of a survey on relevant policy research
reflected a lack of sufficient or cohesive community policies to help reduce underage access to
alcohol.  She explained the Partnership’s strategic plan on this topic and efforts to forward the issue
to state legislators; advised of a forthcoming report to be released later this year; provided statistical
information relative to research involving underage drinking, and encouraged the governing body
to not move forwa rd with the ordinance at this time. Br yan Grzegorczyk spoke in favor  of the
ordinance, stating employed individuals are not going to work to drink, but to work.  Mr. Ya tes
expressed his belief that the proposed ordinance change was a workforce problem, not an underage
drinking i ssue, and commented on liquor licensee s’ financ ial investments in their respe ctive
businesses.  Mr. Col lins moved to appr ove on sec ond reading, seconded by Mr. B rown. Upon
inquiry, Tom Montoya, Chief Enforcement Officer, Wyoming Liquor Division, provided a brie f
history involving the adoption of the 2007 state legislative bill, explaining the bill was originated
based upon a decreasing labor force in certain areas of the state due to effects of the energy boom.
He indicated the proposed ordinance will affect liquor licensees operating in a restaurant and full-
service restaurant environment only, and will not apply to bars (i.e. Licensee operations not meeting
the state definition of restaurant and full service restaurants).  In addition, he stated it will not allow
18, 19 and 20 year olds to serve as bartenders, and reported that, state-wide, L iquor D ivision
enforcement agents have reported no problems with 18-20 yr. old employees serving.  Extensive
discussion followed and included the following subjects: Amending municipal code to be in line
with state statutes; workforce labor shortages; suggestion of requesting state legislators to change
the definition of dispensing room; raising wages to attract employees; current state-wide “Where Do
We Draw The Line” program; access to alcohol and underage drinking issues; exposure of alcohol
to those under 21 y ears of age by allowing them to serve in dispensing  rooms; food and a lcohol
service areas; current employee services within vs. outs ide of a  dispensing r oom; bartender and
server T IPS t raining r equirements an d P olice u nderage co mpliance ch ecks; z ero u nderage
access/drinking tolerance policy; liquor licensees’ responsibilities involving underage individuals,
and other City ordinances approved to assist with alcohol monitoring and control.  Upon inquiry
during comments, City Clerk Carol Intlekofer clarified the governing body reviews annual liquor
licenses (renewal applications) after the first of each year before the renewal term begins, which is
April 1 .  U pon in quiry, M s. Svo bode c larified i ndividuals s erving a lcohol t o c ustomers inst
restaurants are responsible for checking IDs, and Police Chief Bob Fecht advised underage alcohol
compliance checks are conducted through the Police Dept. as well as review of employee TIPS
training records. He stated throughout the past year no TIPS violations have been found at any of the
facilities. During discussion and upon re quest by Chief Fecht, the Chief, with assistanc e by Mr.
Skala, presented a “what if” scenario reflecting the difference between wait staff serving food and
alcohol “inside” vs. “outside” a dispensing room. Further discussion followed on fines for underage
serving and licensee/managerial responsibility for compliance with state and local laws.  Motion
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the exception of Ms. Broyles, Mr.
Pierson and Mr. Valdez voting “no”. Under other City business, Amber Ash spoke in favor of the
ordinance; suggested Coun cil review what other  states ar e allowing; cited her  former personal
employment experience relative to the  ordinance topic, and expressed her belief that it i s t he
bartenders’ role to oversee a dispensing room area, which is more closely monitored than other areas
of a restaurant environment.

Mr. Seg rave moved to amend by  substitut e, which c larifies f unding sour ces f or the purc hase,
seconded by Mr. Brown.  Ri ck Parish, Parks and Recreation Director, advised the substitute, in
addition to clarifying dollar amounts and funding sources as requested at Finance Committee, also
contains min or wo rding c hanges.  Mo tion to  a mend by su bstitute c arried.  Vo ting “ yes” – a ll
members of the governing body.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Parish provided information on the design-build
contract  previously approved for the design and construction of a new Parks maintenance building
at D utcher F ield; e xplained c osts a ffiliated w ith the  ne w b uilding, infrastructure a nd sit e
improvements (approximately $1.2 milli on), and r eviewed fundin g sources and aspe cts of and
amounts expended toward the design-build contract. He stated the Parks shop was being displaced
due to the new  Children’s Discovery Garden to be built at B otanic Gardens and that during  the
course of seeking a temporary facility to lease until the new building could be constructed, the Dell
Range property was broug ht to his attention.  He stated the De ll Range property consists of a
building (14,000 sq. ft.) that is larger than the one proposed to be constructed (7,000 sq. ft.) and
includes 2.9 ac res of  land, providing  for a dditional future expansion, for a  purcha se price  of
$1,000,000.00.  He noted positive characteristics of the site (i.e. ready access to major roadways,
room for equipment storage, opportunity for growth and enhancements at the site).  D iscussion
followed and included the following topics: Time frame affiliated with new construction and locating
a temporary site for the maintenance shop vs. decision process to instead propose purchasing a
facility; original placement of a new facility at Dutcher Field as developed during preliminary plans;
costs affiliated with the design-build contract that the City must pay under the contract; benefits of
the Dell Range property, including space for growth; cost comparison of purchasing the property vs.
constructing a new facility; concern with proposed change of constructing a facility to purchasing
an existing property, and communication with City Council members by staff on the proposal to
purchase.  Mr. Parish reviewed the expenses that would be involved in construction of a new facility
at Dutcher Field and noted that the property has depreciated value (which would include the new
building if constructed) as Land and Water Conservation grant funds have been received in the past,
making the property unable to be readily disposed of (sold) if desired by the City in the future.  Brad
Carnahan, Director of Parks, confirmed the architect’s original estimate to construct the new building
was in excess of $900,000.00.  F urther discussion followed in suppor t as well as non- support of
purchasing the  proper ty vs. con structing a new  fac ility; benef its of a “sing le move” f or Parks
involving gas tanks, equipment and staff; potential site amenities that could be developed at the Dell
Range property and amount of inside a nd outside storag e spac e ava ilable, and a dministrative
management issues.  Main motion as amended by substitute carried.  Voting “yes” – all members
of the governing body with the exception of Ms. Case and Mr. Laybourn voting “no”.  (#5063)

bid of Ameri-Tech Equipment Co., Casper, in an amount not to exceed $245,480.00, seconded by
Mr. Brown.  Upon inquiry pursuant to a letter  that had been  received from another bidder, Rudy
Apodaca, Director of Purchasing, advised state statutes were followed in determining the proposed
successful bidder, clarifying that Ameri-Tech Equipment has been a long standing dealer for Teem,
and qualifies as a r esident bidder, therefore, the 5% in-state  preference was applied. Mr. Brown
clarified for  the re cord tha t his personal business company  (Clerk’s note : Ameri-Te ch HVA C
Services) is not associated with Ameri-Tech Equipment of Casper. Motion carried.  Voting “yes”
– all members of the governing body.

Appointment of  Ami Skeens to the Public Transit Advisory Board, and Peter Illoway to the
Cheyenne Regional Airport Boa rd.  Mr. Collins moved to approve, se conded by Mr. Seg rave.
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. 
ANNOUNCEMENTS/REPORTS/MOTIONS:
Annual leaseholder improvement report submitted by the Airport Golf Club, I nc. Bryan
Grzegorczyk, President of Airport Golf Club, indicated things have been running smoothly at the
Club and they have had no problems.  Mr. Seg rave moved to acknowledge receipt of the annual
report, seconded by Mr. Brown.  Upon inquiry concerning recent building roof improvements, Mr.
Grzegorczyk advised the City had the roof replaced as it is responsible for improvements outside of
the building; the Club takes care of improvements within the building.  He reviewed interior areas
that had been remodeled or improved within the past year and, upon query concerning effect on the
Club’s business due to the No Smoking ordinance, advised pull tab (“pickle”) and drink sales were
“down some”, stating tha t proceeds from pickle sales g oes toward Club scholarships.  Motion
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.
Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held August 25, 2008 at 6:00 p.m. in City
Council Chambers to discuss the submission of an application for grant funding to the Wyoming
Business Council under the Business Ready Communities: Community Enhancements Program for
funding for continued development of David R. Romero South Cheyenne Community Park.
Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held August 25, 2008 at 6:00 p.m. in City
Council Chambers for a restaurant liquor license application filed with the City Clerk's Office for
Primos Cheyenne d/b/a Scotty's Pizza Factory, 1802 Dell Range, Cheyenne, WY.
OTHER CITY BUSINESS:
Resident John Fafoutakis discussed the completion time frame for the new roadway located
by the main Post Office (off Converse) and its benefits to traffic flow in the area; suggested clean-up
efforts be conducted in the drainage ditches at the Rue Terre/Dell Range intersection; encouraged
placement of more plastic recycling bins in the south Cheyenne area; questioned clean-up and costs
of recent flooding that occurred at the Civic Center, and expressed his displeasure with a recent letter
to the editor published in the newspa per written by a City  Council member involving a nother
Council member relative to the upcoming primary election.  With regard to more plastic recycling
bins, Mayor Spiker noted that the Director of Solid Waste has not yet been successful in locating any
business owners in the south Cheyenne area who were interested in having bins on their property.
He also reviewed the City’s property insurance coverage generally with regard to the Civic Center
flooding and advised alternate options for managing drainage near the facility are being researched
to hopefully alleviate future re-occurrences.
Resident Jennifer Price discussed an issue involving herself and a neighbor pursuant to dog
barking and disturbance of the peace, and suggested City Code be changed to support dog owners.
Upon inquiry, City Attorney Claudia Angelos confirmed a former mediation program, implemented
through the City Attorney’s office a few years ago, was no longer available as they were unable to
find individuals to provide mediation service s at no cost.  Comment was made in support of a
mediation program.  Mayor Spiker suggested Ms. Price visit with Police Chief Fecht, who was in
attendance, regarding some of her concerns.
 
Miscellaneous c omments by  City  Council members include d: Civi c Center  insurance
coverage; recent increase in sanitation tipping fees at the landfill relative to notification efforts to
businesses providing sanitation pick-up services to County residents; appreciation to staff for efforts
spent in Civic Center clea n-up of f lood water s and mit igation attempts to alleviate da mage;
suggestion that the Board of Public Utilities consider locating its new administration/engineering
building within t he downtown a rea instead of 24  St. (and Sny der), and announc ement of theth
availability of free counseling services for certain military veterans and families provided by Peak
Wellness Center.
There being  no further  business to come bef ore the  governing body , the meeting  was
officially adjourned at 10:41 p.m.
 Submitted by,
 
 Carol A. Intlekofer, MMC
 City Clerk
 
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request.
Published: Wyoming Tribune-Eagle
 August 22, 2008

MEETING: Cheyenne City Council | DATE: 2008-08-25 | CLIP_ID: 41 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 41

 RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
August 25, 2008
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date at 6:00 p.m.
in Council Chambers.  Present were: MAYOR-Jack R. Spiker; COUNCIL MEMBERS-Mr. Brown,
Ms. Broyles, Ms. Case, Mr. Collins, Mr. Laybourn, Mr. Pierson, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Segrave and Mr.
Valdez.
 
Also present: Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk; Barb Dorr, City Treasurer, and Claudia Angelos,
City Attorney.
Consent Agenda. (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to be
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests, in which event the item
will be removed from the Consent Agenda and considered in its normal sequence on the agenda.)
Upon request by Mr. Laybourn, Mayor Spiker announced agenda items #20, 21, 22, 31(a), 32(b) and
33(a) would be removed from the Consent Agenda.  Comment was made pertaining to removal of
Consent Agenda items.  Mr. Collins moved to approve remaining Consent Agenda items, seconded
by M r. B rown.  M otion c arried.  V oting “yes” –  a ll members o f t he g overning bo dy w ith t he
exception of Mr. Laybourn voting “no”.
[CA] MINUTES FROM REGULAR MEETING OF THE GOVERNING BODY ON AUGUST
11, 2008.  Motion (per consent agenda) carried.
[CA] MINUTES FROM VOUCHERS MEETING OF THE GOVERNING BODY ON AUGUST
18, 2008.  Motion (per consent agenda) carried.
Mr. Segrave  moved to approve payment of vouchers as presented, seconded by Mr. Valdez.
Upon inquiry, City Treasurer Barb Dorr and Capt. Jeff Schulz, Cheyenne Police Dept., reviewed
information available on a voucher payable to Cornerstone Programs Corp. ($10,000.00), juvenile
detention fees for month of July.  Capt. Schulz advised Cornerstone operates the facility and provides
services, but Wardell Academy owns the property, and provided information on services received,
police monitoring, and financial support for services involving the City, Sheriff’s Dept. and State of
Wyoming Dept. of Family Services.  He stated there is no other local facility at the present time to
house juvenile offenders. Comments followed on operational management of the facility and past
problems when the facility was operated by Wardell Academy.  Up on query, Ms. Dorr reviewed
vouchers payable to Ft. Collins Radiator ($100.00), radiator repair; H.W. Lochner ($12,429.31),
Crow Creek Flood Control improvement project engineering inspection (Warren Ave. bridge), and
Lands End, Inc. ($32.45), clothing purchase.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the
governing body with the exception of Mr. Laybourn voting “no”.
Mayor Spiker opened a public hearing to receive input and discussion on the submission of
an appl ication f or gr ant f unding t o t he W yoming Bus iness Co uncil under t he B usiness Re ady
Communities: Community Enhancements Program for funding for continued development of David
R. Romero South Cheyenne Community Park. Teresa Moore, Parks and Recreation Dept., conducted
a brief visual presentation involving the purpose of the grant funding request of $500,000.00 (50/50
match) and public meetings that were held to receive input on the Park’s conceptual master plan.  She
advised the grant request is to assist with Phase I development (i.e. sidewalks, trails, playground,
plaza and enhancements to picnic shelter), and provided schedule information for completion of Phase
I.  There being no further comments or input and purpose of the hearing having been met, Mayor
Spiker closed the hearing.
Mayor Spiker opened a public hearing for the purpose of receiving comments and input for
a restaurant liquor license application filed with the City Clerk's Office for Primos Cheyenne d/b/a
Scotty's Pizza Factory, 1802 Dell Range, Cheyenne, WY.  There being no comments or input and
purpose o f t he hearing having been met, Mayor Spiker closed t he hearing. The application was
referred to Finance Committee.
“AN OR DINANCE A MENDING TI TLE 5,  BU SINESS LICENSE S A ND
REGULATIONS, C HAPTER 5 .12, A LCOHOLIC BEVERAGES, S ECTION 5 .12.090 (B ),
MINORS-DISPENSING R OOM REST RICTIONS, O F THE CODE OF  T HE CIT Y OF
CHEYENNE, WYOMING, CONCERNING MINORS EMPLOYED IN A RESTAURANT AND
FULL SERVICE RESTAURANT ENVIRONMENT.”  Speaking in opposition to the ordinance
were: Sharon Guerney, affiliated with the State’s Enforcing Underage Drinking Laws program; Jim
Cook, parent and Vice-President of Student Services for Laramie County Community College; Kelsey
James and Ryan Regaglia, Central High School students, and Alfrieda Gonzales, Executive Director,
Laramie County Community Partnership.  Topics discussed included: Information on “Regulating
Strategies for Preventing Youth Access to Alcohol: Best Practices” pursuant to th e U.S. Dept. of
Justice’s E nforcing U nderage D rinking La ws P rogram; p reventing y outh ac cess t o a lcohol;
enforcement of underage drinking violations by law officers; belief that the proposed ordinance is not
a workforce issue but one of business convenience affecting public safety and health; developmental
differences between persons18 to 21 years of age; general statistics involving youth alcohol use and
alcohol related injuries, accidents and incidents; culture and alcohol environment; LCCC alcohol
survey results; economic improvement in salaries paid to young adults working in service related
restaurant environments as an employment incentive; educational awareness and promotion through
responsible training programs; exposure to and availability of alcohol to persons under the legal
drinking age;  co mmunity m essage o f i ntolerance f or un derage dri nking; i ssues, i ncluding peer
pressure, involving 18 to 21 year olds serving alcohol in restaurant dining areas as currently allowed
vs. serving of food and alcohol within a dispensing room environment; recent review of alcohol
regulations in place throughout Laramie County by the Laramie County Community Partnership and
anticipated r eport t o b e r eceived in Oct ober from t he S tate Dep t. o f Health, S ubstance Abuse
Division, reflecting a similar review of state statutes affiliated with underage drinking concerns; need
for a coherent community policy, and request to the governing body to allow time to receive the state
report.  The following individuals, affiliated with City retail and bar and grill type liquor licenses,
spoke in favor of the ordinance: Rachel Svoboda, Old Chicago; Jay Hutchinson, Olive Garden; Mylon
Skala, Applebees, and John Johnson, Old Chicago.  T opics discussed included: Workforce labor
shortage issues; employee wages; inability of persons 18 to 21 years old to serve food and alcohol
to patrons within a dispensing room vs. current City Code and state law allowing those employees
who are at least 18 years of age (in a restaurant environment as defined by state statute) to serve
alcohol to all other dining areas of the restaurant; clarification that the definition of “serving” does
not include mixing and pouring drinks (bartending); importance of being able to utilize persons ages
18-21 as a labor source; labor pool shortage issues affecting customer service levels, and licensees’
responsibilities, i ncluding e mployee T IPS t raining and e nforcement o f bu siness p olicies, a nd
compliance with laws.  John Fafoutakis expressed his belief that the problem is not with restaurant
environments or underage drinking, but with the irresponsibility generally of the alcohol industry,
particularly i ts adv ertising t echniques. Dur ing co mments, v arious s peakers s ubmitted wri tten
documentation (on file with office of City Clerk) to governing body members in support of their
statements. Marty Hendrickson, Restaurant and Lodging Assoc., read a letter on behalf of Lynn
Birleffi, Executive Director of the Wyoming Lodging and Restaurant Assoc., expressing Ms. Birleffi’s
support of the ordinance and its importance in assisting restaurant owners’ with workforce challenges
and quality of customer services.  George Halyak questioned the ordinance’s purpose and meaning.
Upon inquiry, City Clerk Carol Intlekofer re-read the entitlement of the ordinance and explained, if
the ordinance is approved, the amendment will affect employees 18 to 21 years of age when employed
only in a restaurant and full service restaurant environment, as defined by state statute, during the
course o f t heir e mployment d uties by a llowing t hem t o p rovide e mployment s ervices w ithin a
dispensing room area in addition to other designated dining areas outside of the dispensing room. Mr.
Halyak discussed the different types of employees that may be allowed within the dispensing room
and suggested modifying the ordinance to be more specific. Reporting for Public Services Committee,
Mr. Collins advised there w as no r ecommendation from th e Committee. Mr. Brown moved to
approve on third and final reading, seconded by Ms. Case.  Upon inquiry, Ms. Intlekofer reviewed
language currently contained in City Code Section 5.12.090 (i.e. sub-sections A. and B.), not just the
portions of sub-section B. involving the proposed amendment.  Extensive discussion followed and
included the following subjects: Underage drinking and workforce labor topics constituting 2 different
issues; l abor sh ortage a nd b usiness co ncerns; curr ent em ployee s ervices wi thin v s. o utside a
dispensing room; ordinance change not pertaining to those liquor licensee establishments whose
primary business purpose involves bars and/or package liquor sales and not the sale of food; proposed
amendment intended to apply to 18-21 year olds while performing employment services only, and
past city ordinances approved by the governing body regulating alcohol issues.  Mr. Collins moved
to amend Section 5.12.090, sub-section B. as  follows, seconded by Dr. Rinne:  “EXCEPT FOR
EMPLOYEES W HO, P URSUANT TO T HE P ERFORMANCE OF  T HEIR EMPL OYMENT
DUTIES, AND WHO ARE AT LEAST EIGHTEEN (18) YEARS OF AGE AND EMPLOYED BY
A LICENSEE IN A RESTAURANT AND FULL SERVICE RESTAURANT ENVIRONMENT,
AS DEFINED BY WYO. STAT. SECTION 12-1-101 (a)(xiv), Nno person under the legal drinking
age may enter or remain in a dispensing room where alcoholic liquors and/or malt beverages are sold
or dispensed.”  Motion t o a mend c arried. V oting “ yes” –  a ll members o f t he g overning b ody.
Discussion continued on employer supervision and responsibilities; police inspections; description of
a di spensing ro om; pas sing addi tional l aws t o h elp wi th en forcement o f  un derage dri nking;
information on  r ecommended a ge of  s ervers vs. minors i n p ossession ( unless i ncidental to
employment) contained in the “Best Practices” information previously submitted during discussion;
other Wyoming municipalities’ laws and State Sheriff’s and Chiefs of Police association’s position
on the issue; employee wages; industry and labor market conditions; amendment affecting employees
working as hosts, hostesses, servers and table busers, and youth responsibility.  Upon inquiry, Police
Capt. Jeff Schulz advised there have not been any serious law enforcement problems with alcohol
service in restaurant environments regarding service to minors.  Mr. Pierson moved to amend Section
5.12.090, sub-section B. to change the age from 18 to at least 19 years of age, seconded by Ms.
Broyles. It was advised raising the age to 19 would eliminate the majority of high school aged persons
from being able to serve alcohol or enter the dispensing room.  Upon inquiry on why the state’s legal
drinking age had been raised in the past from 19 to 21 years old, Captain Schulz clarified it was based
upon the State not losing highway funding.  Motion to amend failed.  Voting “yes” – Ms. Broyles,
Mr. Pierson, Dr. Rinne, and Mayor Spiker.  Voting “no” – Mr. Brown, Ms. Case, Mr. Collins, Mr.
Laybourn, Mr. Segrave and Mr. Valdez.  Mr. Pierson moved to amend Section 5.12.090 to add a new
subsection C . t hat woul d re ad: “ ALL SER VERS UN DER THE A GE O F 21  M UST C EASE
SERVICE IN  TH E D ISPENSING AREA AFTER 9 :00 P.M .”, s econded by Ms. B royles. M r.
Hutchinson spoke in opposition to the 9:00 p.m. deadline and explained the restaurant he’s affiliated
with ( i.e. O live G arden) c loses a t 1 0:00 p .m. a nd th e e ffect th e a mendment w ould h ave on
management o f s taffing l evels an d cus tomer se rvice.  Upo n inquiry, Ca pt. Sch ulz pro vided
information on the City’s curfew hours for minors.  Motion to amend failed.  Voting “yes” – Mr.
Pierson and Mayor Spiker.  Voting “no” –  Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Ms. Case, Mr. Collins, Mr.
Laybourn, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Segrave and Mr. Valdez.  Further discussion included workforce issues,
minimum wage and salary levels for wait staff, living wages within t he community,18 year o lds
serving in the military and being able to vote, exposure to alcohol and underage drinking concerns,
and deferring action on the ordinance to allow time for the report from the state to be completed to
provide po ssible guidance on t he issue.  Ms . Bro yles moved t o po stpone to Oct ober 27, 2008,
seconded by Mr. Valdez. Motion to postpone carried.  Voting “yes” – Ms. Broyles, Ms. Case, Mr.
Laybourn, Mr. Pierson, Mr. Valdez and Mayor Spiker.  Voting “no” –  Mr. Brown, Mr. Collins, Dr.
Rinne and Mr. Segrave.
Following a 10 minute recess, Mayor Spiker reconvened the meeting at 8:36 pm.
“AN ORDINANCE ANNEXING TO THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, PUBLIC

AND 3618 RAWLINS STREET).”  Mr. Collins moved to approve on second reading, seconded by

“AN ORDINANCE VACATING MCCOMB AVENUE BETWEEN W. LINCOLNWAY
AND W. 17  STREET AND THE 16-FOOT ALLEY WITHIN BLOCK 401, ORIGINAL CITY,TH
CHEYENNE, WYOMING (LOCATED BETWEEN MISSILE DRIVE AND MCCOMB AVENUE
AND BETWEEN W. LINCOLNWAY AND W. 17  STREET).”  Mr. Collins moved to approveTH
on second reading with Planning Commission condition #1 , seconded by Mr. Brown. Upon inquiry,
Martin Matsen, Development Director, provided information on the location of the property relative
to the Microtel motel project, and Jim Hancock, Wyoming Grand, provided property ownership
information.  Motion carried. Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.

“AN OR DINANCE A MENDING TI TLE 17 , Z ONING, CH APTER 17.08,  Z ONING
DISTRICTS ESTABLISHED AND SECTION 17.08.040 CHEYENNE CITY MAP O F T HE
CHEYENNE CITY CODE 2002, BY CHANGING THE ZONING CLASSIFICATION FROM LR-
1 LOW-DENSITY RESIDENTIAL-ESTABLISHED DISTRICT TO MUR MIXED USE WITH
RESIDENTIAL E MPHASIS D ISTRICT FOR T HE E AST 6 5-FEET O F LOT 1 4, BLOCK 3,
YELLOWSTONE A DDITION, CH EYENNE, W YOMING (LO CATED NOR TH OF  A ND
ADJACENT TO DELL RANGE BOULEVARD, EAST OF YELLOWSTONE ROAD).”  Ron
Pinther, agent for the applicant, spoke in favor of the ordinance and advised the reason for the zone
change request is due to proposed purchase of the property for light business use; discussed similar
zoning uses in the area for insurance and real estate offices, and cited sections from the PlanCheyenne
document relative to converting residential homes to small business uses in the respective area.  He
reviewed discussion and procedures at the Planning Commission’s meeting on the item, including his
concerns w ith p rocedures i nvolving p resentation o f information, and t he C ommission’s
recommendation to deny the zone request; submitted printed information in support of his statements
(documents on file with office of City Clerk), and stated he had contacted property owners on either
side of the property and both support the zone change request. Mr. Pinther clarified the houses
adjacent to the property are currently residences, not businesses. The following residents in the area
spoke in opposition to the zone change:  R osalind Schliske, Cindy Paul, Karleen Dagit, Eugene
Knipping and Darlene McClure.  T opics discussed included: History of Yellowstone Addition as
initially developed; maintaining the integrity and residential character of the established neighborhood;
previous re-zoning attempts; spot zoning; aesthetic green space provided by the Airport’s property
containing the elk sculpture; re-zoning effect on the neighborhood; buffering and transitioning of
residential areas to retail use; future planning for use of the area; alley access concerns and increased
traffic; compromised neighborhood due to other businesses developed in the neighborhood; Oxford
House rehabilitation program in the neighborhood that has resulted in additional traffic and safety
concerns, and alley drainage conditions.  Mr. Pinther spoke in support of comments made suggesting
the City conduct long range planning to determine future allowable uses in the area, commented on
declining property values, and responded to comments involving his potential interest in acquiring the
Oxford Ho use pro perty.  Upo n i nquiry pert aining t o M r. Pi nther’s reques t at  Pub lic Serv ices
Committee to consider referring the item back to the Planning Commission for further consideration
and whether the governing body had a past precedent involving referral of agenda items back to the
Commission, City Attorney Claudia Angelos stated she believed the governing body could send the
item back to the Commission if desired, or the body could either approve or deny the zoning request.
Upon request, City Clerk Carol Intlekofer read a letter (on file with office of City Clerk) that was
issued to Mr. Pinther from the Development office relative to the process involved in having the item
reconsidered o r r eviewed f urther by  t he P lanning C ommission.  M artin M atsen, D evelopment
Director, noted the difference between having an item reconsidered by the Planning Commission vs.
a request for further review or evaluation on an item by the Commission, and provided information
on Commission policies relative to reconsideration vs. additional review or evaluation.  He stated if
the ordinance was “voted down” by the governing body, the applicant would have the ability to ask
the Planning Commission to reconsider the item at its next meeting if a Commission member who
voted on the prevailing side brings it up for discussion. During comments on the PlanCheyenne
document, Mr. Matsen advised he did not see a conflict with the Plan and the area being discussed
as it is a broader area that includes secondary uses and it was not the intent of the Plan to change
existing neighborhoods.  Bran don Cammarata, Planning Dept., addressed t he perceived conflict
regarding PlanCheyenne a nd s tated re sidential c onversions a long a rterial s treets i s t o e nsure
neighborhoods will be protected to be livable and to address access issues on a very busy road while
encouraging investment in properties that sometimes don’t see investment due to a particular use.
Mr. Collins advised there was no recommendation on the item from Public Services Committee.  Mr.
Segrave moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. Pierson. Upon inquiry regarding
other properties in the respective area that are now being utilized as businesses, Mr. Matsen clarified
there are two additional properties conducting businesses out of residential structures that were re-
zoned to MUR (Mixed Use with Residential Emphasis), and indicated other zone changes along the
corridor have been requested in the past that have not been approved. He advised if the ordinance
moved forward, the driveway along Dell Range should be closed with the alley providing access to
the property. Discussion followed on a past zone change request, concerns with property ingress and
egress, long range planning involving the neighborhood, and a conceptual plan for the area to address
future changes.  Mo tion failed. Vo ting “yes” – Mr . Laybourn.  Vo ting “no” – Mr . Brown, Ms.
Broyles, Ms. Case, Mr. Collins, Mr. Pierson, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Segrave, Mr. Valdez and Mayor Spiker.
Following a ten minute recess, Mayor Spiker reconvened the meeting at 10:16 pm.

PRELIMINARY PLATS/PRELIMINARY ZONE CHANGES:
City Preliminary Plat for Sundance Ridge II, a r eplat of Tracts 2 an d 3, Skyline Tracts,
Cheyenne, W yoming ( located b etween S torey Boulevard a nd S kyline D rive, on  b oth s ides of
Sycamore Road).  Ed Clark, resident in the vicinity, recited the many changes in the neighborhood
that h ave o ccurred al ong St orey Bl vd. i n recen t y ears and s poke i n f avor o f t he pro posed
development with the following reservations and concerns: Requests the developer provide property
covenants and agreements to eliminate overhead utilities to protect the view, and require off-street
parking o nly due to safety issues.  Ap ril B rimmer Ku nz, r esident in t he vicinity, r eviewed t he
residential ownership and non-commercial nature of the area on the north side of Storey Blvd. relative
to developer plans to build 10 town homes beneath the ridge on Skyline Drive.  She indicated she was
not opposed to the town homes, but expressed concerns with potential commercial business growth
that may be allowed on the north side o f Storey Blvd. and unknown use o f future vacant lots,
including the large county pocket area.  She urged review of the whole neighborhood relative to
future p roperty u ses a nd z oning l aws be  c onducted, a nd that water a nd s ewer a ccesses a nd
connection costs t o p otential future p roperty o wners a nnexing into t he City be d etermined a nd
information provided. She advised that she had heard it would cost $75,000 to access the sewer line
on Powderhouse Road (installed during The Pointe subdivision project); noted information she had
received from the Board of Public Utilities reflected the cost to tap into the sewer line would be
$12,000, an d s tated all t he issues raised s hould b e rev iewed b efore t he properties are pl atted,
particularly to determine a reasonable price for water and sewer services to those property owners
who ma y wish to annex in the future.  Mr. Collins moved to acknowledge receipt with s taff
recommendations #1, 2 and 3, seconded by Ms. Broyles.  Upon inquiry, information was provided
on the staff recommendations.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.
City Preliminary Plat for Gateway South, a replat of a portion of Tract 1, Allison Tracts and
in the NW1/4 of Section 8, T.13N., R.66W., 6th P.M., Cheyenne, Wyoming (located at the southeast
corner of the intersection of Walterscheid Boulevard and W. Fox Farm Road).  J ohn Fafoutakis
expressed c oncerns w ith a ntelope in t he a rea a nd q uestioned w hether t he he rd c ould s afely be

through 4, seconded by Ms. Broyles.  U pon inquiry, it was advised that pursuant to input at the
Public Services Committee meeting, the South Cheyenne Water and Sewer District will provide water
and sewer services to the area as it is part of the District’s original boundaries.  Comment was made
on d etermination by the D istrict r elative t o w hen it will or will not p rovide services t o c ertain
properties.  It  was also noted that staff recommendation #5 had been resolved.  Mo tion carried.
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the exception of Mr. Segrave voting “no”.

LEASES/CONTRACTS/LEGAL:
Development Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and the Church of St. Mary for the
cooperative construction of street improvements at the intersection of 22  Street and O’Neil Avenuend
in conjunction with the site development work at 2200 O’Neil Avenue  (2003-2006 1% Sales Tax).
Tony Lucero, qu estioned use of 1% Sales Tax funds for the project. Ken Lewis, City Engineer,
responded to the inquiry by reviewing 1% projects and providing information on maintenance work
required in the area due to the road’s high crown, which poses vehicular problems and which will
eventually require an overlay.  He indicated the agreement is fiscally cost efficient by partnering with
St. Mary’s to achieve the work the City needs to do during the time frame that St. Mary’s is installing
its required infrastructure in the street in conjunction with its new school.  Mr. Lucero questioned
whether lo wering t he c rown w ill d ivert w ater a nd a ffect flo oding c onditions w ith M r. L ewis
explaining that lowering the crown will assist with lessening water capacity in the gutters and will not
significantly alter the water flow around the school.  Randy Byers, architect for St. Mary’s School
project, provided further information on the street crown, which is currently a challenge to individuals
with disabilities, and stated the Church’s support of partnering with the City on the project.  George
Halyak expressed concerns with the proposed construction and discussed the following issues he felt
should be resolved in association with the project: Street drainage; increased traffic at the school’s
drop-off/pick-up area, traffic flow/volume generally and turning area; possible alley closure; whether
a bump-out design is needed, and the Church’s future school project would request vacation of 22nd
St. in the future.  Mr. Byers reviewed construction improvements proposed, including the bump-out
design (pedestrian safety), and stated access plans to and from the school have not changed, and the
alley will not be closed or impeded.  Mr. Segrave moved to approve in an amount not to exceed
$86,946.50, seconded by Mr. Collins.  Upon inquiry regarding whether a traffic flow study had been
conducted, Mr. Byers advised a traffic study had been completed and approved as part of the site plan
review process and provided further information on the street  improvements and reviewing agencies
providing c omments d uring t he site p lan review a s w ell a s o ther a gencies a nd businesses being
contacted during the project.  Upon inquiry, Capt. Jeff Schulz advised the police department did not
have any issues with the site plan. In response to inquiry  regarding use of 1% funds for the project,
Vicki Nemecek, Assistant Public Works Director, clarified the City’s funding for the project will be
from t he 1%  st reet r enovation l ine i tem an d wi ll n ot t ake f unding aw ay f rom o ther pr ojects.
Discussion followed on the opportunity to partner with St. Mary’s for reduced costs to the City, use
of funds from the 1% street renovation line item, repair of the street crown and effect of reducing
street water volume, street crowning conditions on other streets, concern with bump-outs, site plan
design for t he s chool, a nd p ortion of the project involving t he C ity’s financial investment a nd
maintenance.  M r. Lewis c larified a pproval o f t he site p lan is a n administrative a ction, a nd t he
drawing before City Council is part of the site plan.  Further discussion followed on the site plan in
conjunction with the roadway, and any future changes to the plans, including whether there will be
a street vacation request by the Church in the future. Mr. Byers advised he could not speak on behalf
of St. Mary’s concerning any future property acquisition and development plans, nor was it germane
to the agenda topic; stated the project will improve handicap and pedestrian access and reviewed the
physical street areas St. Mary’s would be paying for vs. the City.  Mr. Lewis clarified if a street
vacation should be requested in the future, the City’s financial investment in the project would not
be lost.  Upon inquiry regarding the school’s playground area, Mr. Byers advised a playground will
be located on the north side for the younger children and a playground on the block to the south will
also be developed.  M otion carried.  Voting “yes” - all members of the governing body with the
exception of Ms. Case and Mr. Laybourn voting “no”.  (#5294)

OTHER CITY BUSINESS:
John Fafoutakis discussed vandalism that had occurred recently at Botanic Gardens, and
commended Police Officer John Gay for his efforts in educating youth of Internet dangers involving
predators.
Wyoming House Representative Mary Throne, District 11, which is part of Ward I and Ward
III, a dvised a  g roup o f interested r esidents has been w orking d iligently to save several historic
residential structures located near the historic Governors’ mansion and expressed concerns that plans
by the property owner t o remove the structures from the area will have irreparable harm on the
Governors’ mansion as an historic site.  She reviewed the recent denial of a City demolition permit
through the Historic Preservation Board’s process and subsequent appeal to the Building Code Board
of Appeals, which upheld the denial; stated the Governors’ mansion neighborhood is a specially
protected historic area and stated her concerns with issuance of a City moving permit to relocate a
structure and urged the governing body to “slow down” the process to allow time for the group to
come up with options or solutions.  Speaking in support of the City intervening in the moving process
were:  J an Nelson Schroll, who read a letter received from former U.S. Senator Alan Simpson,
requesting  properties in the area be saved to preserve the history of Cheyenne; Nancy Abraham, who
requested dedicated historic districts receive respect and urged the City Council to use its power to
stop the moving of structures to maintain the integrity of the neighborhood, and Mary Ann Taliaferro,
who reviewed the area’s history and prominent individuals that had resided in the homes.  Speakers
requested the governing body intervene in the pending moving of a building within the historic area
to provide time to research options or alternatives; further requested that the governing body revisit
its regulations concerning moving permits relative to historic districts, and also discussed the historic
Governors’ Mansion neighborhood relative to possible sale of property in the area to the hospital that
purportedly may utilize the land for a parking lot.  Comments followed on the intention of the City’s
ordinance designating the Governors’ Mansion as an historic area; property owners’ rights; potential
hospital involvement; significance of area; interpretation of City Code permit requirements, a nd
Mayor and City Council authority to halt moving of a structure following issuance of a moving permit
through the Building Department. Mayor Spiker provided information available on moving permit
application procedures and status of the application that had been filed.  He explained the process a
moving permit applicant has to follow and compliance areas that have to be met.  Discussion included
whether the Mayor or City has legal authority to stop or intervene, either directly or through order
to the Police Chief, the moving process; moving permit application reviews and approvals required;
seeking a written legal review to determine Mayoral and City Council authority and options; process
utilized to review and approve demolition permits for designated historic areas vs. moving permits;
protection of the historic Governors’ Mansion area, and demolishing buildings vs. moving buildings
and effect of each on the historic integrity of the district.  City Attorney Claudia Angelos, stated her
concerns with mayoral and governing body authority to act upon the issue, and commented on state
statute and City Code provisions, which she indicated does not allow for the Mayor to stop the
issuance o f t he m oving p ermit o nce a pplication r equirements ha ve been met a nd a pproved by
necessary departments and the permit is issued.  U pon inquiry on whether there is a time frame
involved in issuing of moving permits, Ken Lewis, City Engineer, advised he was not aware of any
for moving permits. Ms. Throne referenced a section of City Code Chapter 15.50, Historic Design
Guideline (specific to the Governors’ Mansion protective area), regarding relevancy of a certificate
of appropriateness requirement for the property owner.  In conjunction with a question posed on
whether the City could file an injunction (with the court), Ms. Angelos advised if discussion continued
into issues that may involve potential litigation then the governing body needs to move into executive
session.  Following brief comments, Mr. Pierson moved to meet in executive session to discuss a
matter of potential litigation, seconded by Mr. Collins.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members
of the governing body with the exception of Mr. Laybourn voting “no”.  Mayor Spiker announced
the motion carried and members would meet in executive session, not open to the public, in Council
Chambers following a 10 minute recess;  that there may or not be any action taken by the governing
body as a result of the executive session, therefore, the meeting would reconvene and be open to the
public following closure of the executive session. He requested the City Attorney, City Clerk,  City
Engineer and Deputy City Attorney be present at the executive session. (Clerk’s Note: Executive
session began at 12:45 a.m.) Following conclusion of the executive session, Mayor Spiker reconvened
the meeting, open to the public at 1:10 a.m.  Mayor Spiker advised no action would be taken by the
governing body as a res ult of the executive session, but a s pecial meeting of the governing body
would be scheduled for Wed., August 27, 2008 at 12:00 p.m. (Noon) in City Council Chambers to
further review the concerns raised regarding removal of homes in the Governor’s historic district.
The meeting was officially adjourned at 1:13 a.m.
 Submitted by,
 
 Carol A. Intlekofer, MMC
 City Clerk
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request.
Published: Wyoming Tribune-Eagle
 September 20, 2008

 RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
September 8, 2008
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date at 6:00 p.m.
in Council Chambers.  Present were: MAYOR-Jack R. Spiker; COUNCIL MEMBERS-Mr. Brown,
Ms. Broyles, Ms. Case, Mr. Collins, Mr. Laybourn, Mr. Pierson, Dr. Rinne (arriving at 6:12 pm), Mr.
Segrave and Mr. Valdez.  The pledge of allegiance was recited.
 
Also present: Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk; Barb Dorr, City Treasurer, and Claudia Angelos,
City Attorney.
Consent Agenda. (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to be
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests, in which event the item
will be removed from the Consent Agenda and considered in its normal sequence on the agenda.)
Upon request by Mr. Laybourn, Mayor Spiker announced agenda items #5, 6, 7, 24, 29(a)(b)(c),
32(a) and 34(a) would be removed from the Consent Agenda. Comment was made pertaining to
removal of Consent Agenda items.  Mr. Collins  moved to approve remaining Consent Agenda items,
seconded by Mr. Valdez.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present
with the exception of Mr. Laybourn voting “no”.

Finance Committee.

WARD I CONTI NGENCY F UNDS TO T HE DAVID R. ROM ERO SOUT H C HEYENNE

the purpose of the resolution is to provide disclosure by Ward I Council members relative to use of
their ward’s budgeted contingency funds.  M r. Laybourn moved to amend by removing Item #8,
Parliamentarian, listed within the resolution and moving that item’s proposed designated funding
amount of $4,000.00 to Item #6, Parks and Recreation Dept. (Lions Park Rehabilitation) to reflect
a total amount for Item #6 of $8,600.00, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  It was noted the primary purpose
of the funding for Item #6 was for re-vegetation (soil amendment) of the park’s area along Central
Ave.  P ursuant t o co mment s uggesting a ch ange t o t he res olution’s en titlement relative t o t he
proposed amendment, Mr. Laybourn agreed, with concurrence by Mr. Segrave as second to the
motion, to also strike the phrase “and towards a parliamentarian” in the entitlement.  Co mments
followed on: Use of each ward’s discretionary contingency funds without being in the form of a
resolution voted upon by Council members of the other wards, intent and support and non-support
of Item #8 (providing education, through consultant services, on parliamentary procedures), and the
resolution serving as a co urtesy to the public to advise how Ward I monies are being expended.
Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the exception of
Mr. Brown voting “no”.  It was clarified even though Item #4, Botanic Gardens (Children’s Garden),
is a Botanic Gardens Foundation, not a budgeted Parks and Recreation project, the funding to be
allocated ($3,000.00) is for Botanic Gardens to acquire a bench to be placed within the Children’s
Garden.  Motion to approve as amended carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body
with the exception of Mr. Brown and Mr. Segrave voting “no”. (#5071)

moved to approve in the amount of $69,129.00, seconded by Mr. Valdez.  Upon inquiry, Lt. Monti
Allsop, Police Department, advised at the current time the program is adequately funded and includes
grant funds, and briefly explained program services.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of
the governing body.  (#5298)
Agreement between the City of Cheyenne, Laramie County, and Cheyenne Regional Medical
Center for the purpose of creating an Emergency Medical Service Delivery System Joint Powers
Board.  Mr. Segrave moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Valdez.  Upon inquiry, Chief Guy
Cameron, Cheyenne Fire and Rescue Dept., clarified approval of the agreement will create a joint
powers board (JPB) and advised the consultant cost relative to the review and evaluation of the
current delivery system was approximately $89,000.00.  Comments followed on a JPB vs. governing
body oversight of the agreement with the provider of emergency medical delivery system services;
transparency; number o f City J PBs an d o ther b oards, an d improvements t o s ystem s ervices as
recommended by the consultant, which included the formation of a JPB. Chief Cameron stated the
overall goal is to improve the system and meet community needs, and explained overlapping service
areas under the current delivery system structure, citing, as an example, utilization of three different
medical directors that could be consolidated into one director to provide more uniformity and less
duplication.  He indicated the investment in improving the system will help “pull areas together” to
achieve economies of scale, eliminate or reduce fragmentation of services, improve costs and correct
inefficiencies.  During discussion, Chief Cameron spoke further on the purpose and role of the JPB,
which will serve as the foundation for implementing service delivery improvements, and commented
on streamlining the administrative process by having the service provider answer to only “one body”,
JPB’s responsibility to evaluate and select the emergency medical service provider, and positive
partnering of the agencies involved.  He noted that Laramie County Commissioners and  Cheyenne
Regional Medical Center have approved the proposed agreement to create the JPB and discussed the
board’s d uties a nd a ccountability; s tate l aw p rovisions c oncerning J PBs; s cenario o f B oard
dissolution, if it is determined to do so in the future; agreement section addressing whether a party
to the agreement wishes to withdraw from the JPB in the future, and advised an RFP (request for
proposals) f or e mergency m edical d elivery s ervices has been i ssued.  D iscussion i ncluded t he
following topics with additional information provided by Chief Cameron: Meetings held with the
consultant and consultant’s knowledge of delivery service issues; professionalizing emergency medical
system delivery services; improving e mergency medical pre-hospital services t o t he c ommunity;
medical di rector ro le; opt-out c lause i n t he a greement; de signation i n t he a greement o f J PB
membership positions, and specific identification of members and their qualifications; opportunity to
work together with the county to provide quality services to the community; utilization of public
funds and diffusion o f e lected o fficials’ respo nsibility, and JPB accountability.  M otion carried.
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the exception of Ms. Case and Mr. Laybourn
voting “no”.  (#5299)
[CA] Agreement between Laramie County School District No. 1 and the City of Cheyenne, Office
of Youth Alternatives, for counseling services to elementary and secondary age children who are
attending the PEP/TRC Center -- referred to Finance Committee. 
[CA] Agreement between Laramie County School District No. 1 and the City of Cheyenne, Office
of Youth Alternatives, for family counseling for school referred students who are in I.E.P.s -- referred
to Finance Committee.
 
[CA] Agreement between Laramie County School District No. 1 and the City of Cheyenne, Office
of Y outh Alternatives, for family counseling for school r eferred students/families - - r eferred to
Finance Committee. 
[CA] Contract between the City of Cheyenne, Metropolitan Planning Organization, and Quinby
Clune Design for the “Cheyenne Wayfinding Plan Phase II”-- referred to Finance Committee
CHANGE ORDERS/CONTRACT MODIFICATIONS:
Following a five minute recess, Mayor Spiker reconvened the meeting at 8:20 p.m.
Contract Modification No. 1 to Contract No. 5280 (Norris Viaduct) to include bid alternates
#1, 2, 3, and 5.  Speaking in favor of financial investment through contract modification to provide
aesthetic improvements were:  Frank Cole, Cole Shopping Center; Sara Burlingame; Carol Matteson
Pascal; Doran Lummis; Jason Thomas, President, Historic South Side Improvement Association
(HSSIA); Mary Ramirez; Manuel Gallegos and Armand Gallegos.  Topics presented and discussed
included:  G reenway a ccess a nd a rea/community c onnectivity; p roject s upport g enerally;
beautification efforts; community involvement through public input at Norris viaduct project planning
meetings; green areas and amenities; maintaining voters’ trust; creating safe neighborhoods; residents
who have been displaced due to the project; elements of the HSSIA’s neighborhood revitalization
plan (pamphlet submitted and on file with office of City Clerk), and the project’s importance on the
neighborhood.  Mr. Segrave moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Valdez.  Comment followed in
support of the Norris bridge realignment project.  Ms. Broyles moved to amend by striking alternates
#1, 2, 3 and 5 and approving contract modification #1 from Reiman Corp. to include revised bid
alternate #1, aesthetic lighting, in the amount of $329,300.00 and alternate #5[b], color staining of
MSE walls and viaduct, in the amount of $84,219.40 for a total modification amount of $413,519.40;
project to be included in the City’s application to the State Loan and Investment Board (SLIB) in the
next funding cycle, seconded by Mr. Valdez.  Upon inquiry by Mr. Thomas and Ms. Pascal, it was
clarified that alternates #2, aesthetic railing on viaduct approaches, and #3, additional landscaping,
were not included in the motion to amend, but as it is a two year project additional landscaping may
be p roposed t oward t he e nd of the project. I t w as a lso no ted t hat t he p roposed a mendment
incorporates value engineering savings that has resulted due to discussions with the contractor.  Upon
request Jeff Fanning, Engineering Services Director, reviewed drawings o f the proposed railing
included in the base bid vs. a depiction of aesthetic railings, and drawings showing the lighting that
will be provided through the base bid vs. proposed aesthetic lighting.  Ms. Pascal commented on
safety concerns for pedestrians relative to lighting.  Mr. Fanning advised the same lighting effect will
be achieved through the value engineering proposal; described decorative items (i.e. railing a nd
concrete, with stamped pattern, design) already included in the base bid, and advised the base bid
provides f or s ome l andscaping.  D iscussion i ncluded: B asic b id i tems a lready i ncluding m ore
attractive design elements than other projects; stone pilasters being included in the decorative lighting;
concrete staining and stamped pattern; unavailability of decorative railing; estimated project cost
when voters approved t he project vs. t oday’s construction and supply costs; SLIB funding and
correlation to upcoming Council work session to discuss potential SLIB funding requests for various
City pro jects; f iscal res ponsibility; i rrigation i nfrastructure t o s upport an y f uture addi tional
landscaping; o ther p otential f unding m echanisms, i ncluding i nvestment o f p roject f unds, a nd
suggestion that a budget analysis for the entire project be prepared.  Mr. Collins raised a point of
order to clarify discussion earlier in the day at an Investment Committee meeting involved a monetary
estimate as an example of investment funds, not an actual dollar amount.  Comments continued on
bid alternates as initially listed on the agenda; site plan issues; neighborhood meeting t o review
alternates proposed in the motion to amend; support for alternates proposed with the initial approval
of the construction contract, and project management.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Fanning further reviewed
lighting differences in the base bid vs. aesthetic lighting.  Mr. Segrave moved to call for the question
to limit debate, seconded by Mr. Collins.  M otion failed (due to a tie vote).  V oting “yes” – Mr.
Brown, Mr. Collins, Mr. Pierson, Mr. Segrave and Mayor Spiker. Voting “no” – Ms. Broyles, Ms.
Case, Mr. Laybourn, Dr. Rinne and Mr. Valdez.  Following further comment on quality project
components, motion to amend failed (due to a tie vote).  Voting “yes” – Ms. Broyles, Mr. Brown,
Mr. Collins, Mr. Pierson and Mayor Spiker.  Voting “no” – Ms. Case, Mr. Laybourn, Dr. Rinne, Mr.
Segrave and Mr. Valdez.  Mr. Laybourn moved to postpone for two weeks (to Sept. 22 ), secondednd
by M r. V aldez.  M r. T homas, M s. P ascal a nd M r. Manuel G allegos s poke i n f avor o f t he
postponement to allow for public involvement, clarification of proposed alternates, and budgetary and
maintenance issues.  Discussion followed on the purpose of postponement and what would transpire
during the postponement time frame.  Mr. Laybourn raised a point of order that comments being
made were not relevant to the motion on the floor.  Comments continued on postponement.  Mr.
Laybourn moved to call the question to limit debate, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Mo tion failed.
Voting “yes” – Mr. Laybourn, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Segrave and Mayor Spiker.  Voting “no” – Mr. Brown,
Ms. Broyles, Ms. Case, Mr. Collins, Mr. Pierson and Mr. Valdez.  During further discussion on the
motion to p ostpone, a point of information was raised for clarification of procedures relating to
potential amendments to the main motion.  John Fafoutakis commented on the length of discussion
time on the item, and Ms. Pascal and Mr. Manuel Gallegos made statements relative to the motion
to postpone. Comments were made on postponing the item relative to the Council’s work session
scheduled later in the week to r eview proposed projects to s ubmit for SLIB funding.  M otion to
postpone failed.  Voting “yes” – Ms. Case, Mr. Laybourn, Dr. Rinne and Mr. Valdez.  Voting “no”
– Mr. Collins, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Pierson, Mr. Brown, Mr. Segrave and Mayor Spiker.  Mr. Collins
moved t o amend by deleting alternates #1, 2, 3 an d 5 an d substituting alternate #5b, as revised
through value engineering, to add stained concrete in the amount of $84,219.40, seconded by Mr.
Pierson.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Fanning confirmed that under the value engineering proposal for alternate
#5, the concrete staining w ould be  t he s ame q uality a nd c ontent a s t he bi d, j ust a  d ifferent
manufacturer a s t he p rovider.  Ke n Lewis, C ity E ngineer, r equested if t he a mendment, o r a ny
additional a mendments, a re a pproved a s bid a lternates t hat t he M ayor be  d irected t o s ign t he
appropriate contract change order pursuant to any amendment(s). Mr. Collins, with concurrence by
Mr. Pierson as second to the motion, agreed to include Mr. Lewis’ request in the motion to amend.
Brief discussion followed on the procedural process involving the original main motion v s. t he
proposed amendment(s).  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” – Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Ms.
Case, Mr. Collins, Mr. Pierson, Mr. Valdez and Mayor Spiker.  Voting “no” – Mr. Laybourn, Dr.
Rinne and Mr. Segrave.  Mr. Collins moved to amend to include bid alternate #1, revised through
value engineering, in the amount of $329,300.00 for aesthetic lighting, seconded by Mr. Pierson.  It
was noted the alternate includes the pedestrian lighting feature.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – Mr.
Brown, Ms. Broyles, Ms. Case, Mr. Collins, Mr. Pierson, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Valdez and Mayor Spiker.
Voting “no” – Mr. Laybourn and Mr. Segrave voting “no”.  Main motion as twice amended carried.
Voting “yes” – Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Ms. Case, Mr. Collins, Mr. Pierson, Mr. Valdez and Mayor
Spiker.  Voting “no” – Mr.  Laybourn, Dr. Rinne and Mr. Segrave.

Request to extend the time period for one additional year in which retail liquor licensee HEJE,
Inc., d/b/a Outlaw Saloon, 3839 East Lincolnway, Cheyenne, WY, is required to become operational
or open for business pursuant to Wyo. Stat. §12-4-103(a)(iv).  Mayor Spiker advised the item was
being wi thdrawn f rom t he age nda an d reques ted C ity Cl erk Caro l In tlekofer provide f urther
explanation.  Ms. Intlekofer advised that, subsequent to Finance Committee meeting discussion, the
Wyoming Liquor Division was contacted regarding when the initial 2 year time frame for “parking”
the license would expire and it was agreed the initial time frame should not expire until March 31,
2009, therefore, the agenda item (requesting one additional year) at this time is a moot point.  She
advised when the licensee’s application comes before the governing body for the next renewal period,
the licensee will need to show good cause for extending the “parked” time period for the license for
one additional year if they are not operational by then.  She clarified the City Attorney and herself had
contacted the attorney representing HEJE, Inc. to advise the initial two year “parked” expiration date
would not be until March 31, 2009, and it was agreed the licensee’s attorney, City Attorney and City
Clerk would request the item be withdrawn from the agenda.
Announcement was made of a Public Meeting to be held September 22, 2008 at 6:00 p.m. in
City Council Chambers to discuss the use of 2003-2006 1% Optional Sales Tax Overage Funds in
the amount of $147,280.00 for the purpose of reimbursing approved expenditures from the 2007-
2010 Parks & Recreation Department’s 1% Optional Sales Tax Allocation.
Announcement was made of the reappointment of Paul Frauendienst to the Building Code
Board o f Ap peals a nd E lizabeth D avis t o t he G reater C heyenne Ar ts Ad visory Council, t o be
confirmed September 22, 2008.
Announcement was made of the appointment of Richard Shanor to the Community Action
of Laramie County Board, Robert Kanard to the Greenway Advisory Committee, Carrisa Pollard to
the P lanning C ommission, N icole N ovotny t o t he B oard o f Ad justments, J ackie S mith t o t he
Personnel Commission, and Phyllis Sherard to the Housing and Community Development Advisory
Board, to be confirmed September 22, 2008.
OTHER CITY BUSINESS:
John Fafoutakis made statements on the following topics: Timing of a traffic light at 9  St.th
and Hwy 85; private attorney firm expenses for a legal opinion involving moving of buildings within
the historic Governors’ Mansion area, and the City Council’s civility ordinance and comments made
when items are removed from the Consent Agenda.  City Engineer Ken Lewis advised the traffic light
referenced by Mr. Fafoutakis was controlled by the State, but he would ask the traffic engineer to
review the matter with WYDOT to ensure the signal was functioning properly.
Jan Nelson Schroll commented on a private attorney firm’s research on case law involving
moving permits issued for buildings within the historic Governors’ Mansion area and submitted a
copy o f City Co de C hapter 2. 64 pert aining t o t he Historic Prese rvation Bo ard.  Sh e rev iewed
wording contained in chapter sections relative to intent and purpose of the chapter.
Responding t o statements p reviously made u nder o ther City business by Mr. Fafoutakis
concerning removal of consent agenda items, Mr. Segrave commented on his advocacy for efficient
government and his support for a Consent Agenda format; and that he will exercise his rights to
promote efficient government and use of the public’s time.
Mr. Segrave moved to adjourn, seconded by Mr. Brown.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” –
all members of the governing body with the exception of Ms. Case and Mr. Laybourn voting “no”.
The meeting was officially adjourned at 10:30 p.m.
 Submitted by,
 
 Carol A. Intlekofer, MMC
 City Clerk
