MEETING: Cheyenne City Council | DATE: 2008-09-22 | CLIP_ID: 45 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 45

 RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
September 22, 2008
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date at 6:00 p.m.
in Council Chambers.  Present were: MAYOR-Jack R. Spiker; COUNCIL MEMBERS-Mr. Brown,
Ms. Broyles, Ms. Case, Mr. Collins, Mr. Laybourn, Dr. Rinne (arriving at 6:25 p.m.), Mr. Segrave
and Mr. Valdez.  Absent: Mr. Pierson.  The pledge of allegiance was recited.
 
Also present: Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk; Barb Dorr, City Treasurer, and Erik Rasmussen,
Deputy City Attorney.
Consent Agenda. (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA]  are considered to be
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests, in which event the
item will be removed f rom the Consent Agenda and considered in its normal sequence  on the
agenda.)  Upon request by Mr. Collins and Mr. Laybourn, Mayor Spiker announced agenda items
#6, 7, 25(a)(b)(c)(d), 27(b) and 29(a) and (b) would be removed from the Consent Ag enda.  Mr.
Collins moved t o approve remaining Consent Ag enda items, sec onded by Mr. B rown.  Motion
carried.  Voting  “yes” – a ll members of the g overning body present with the exception of Mr.
Laybourn voting “no”.
Mayor Spiker recognized the Chey enne Board of Public Utili ties, and spec ifically Clint
Bassett, BOPU Water Conservation Specialist, for receiving the national WateReuse Association
2008 Public Education Program of the year award for the Cheyenne Water Reuse Program.  Mayor
Spiker presented Mr. Bassett with the award plaque, and noted plaque wording indicating BOPU’s
dedication to the water r euse c ommunity throug h educa tion programs that enhanc e better
appreciation of water resources, management and conservation.
 MINUTES FROM REGUL AR MEETI NG OF  THE GOV ERNING B ODY ON
SEPTEMBER 8, 2008.  Mr. Collins moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Brown.  Motion  carried.
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.
 MINUTES FROM VOUCHERS MEETI NG OF  THE GOV ERNING B ODY ON
SEPTEMBER 15, 2008.  Mr. Brown moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Motion carried.
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.
Mr. Segrave moved to approve payment of vouchers as presented, seconded by Mr. Valdez.
Upon inquiry, City Treasurer Barb Dorr provided information available on the following vouchers
payable to: Chamber of Commerce ($4,617.00), annual City membership dues renewal; Habitat for
Humanity ($60,899.78), reimbursements for Messenger Court infrastructure project ($51,732.64)
and Katrina project ($9,167.14); JM Rodent Control ($3,149.30), rodent control by Weed & Pest at
several facilities and grounds, and Douglas Selby ($6,832.57), concept design for Cheyenne Depot
Plaza (building) project (current fiscal year budget and proposed State Loan and Investment Board
consensus grant funds).  During inquiry, Mayor Spiker provided additional information on Chamber
of Commerce membership benefits, and professional preliminary design services for the Depot Plaza
building.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.
Mayor Spiker opened a public meeting for the purpose of receiving comments and input to
discuss the use of 2003-2006 1% Optional Sales Tax Overage Funds in the amount of $147,280.00
for the pur pose of r eimbursing approved expenditures from the 2007-2010 Par ks & Rec reation
Department’s 1% Optional Sales Tax All ocation.  Todd F eezer, Parks and Rec reation Dept.,
provided information on the purpose of re-allocating the funds due to expenditures affiliated with
the new Parks Shop property recently purchased by the City.  There being no further comments or
input and purpose of the public meeting having been met, Mayor Spiker closed the public meeting.
“AN ORDINANCE VACATING MCCOMB AVENUE BETWEEN W. LINCOLNWAY
AND W. 17  STREET AND THE 16-FOOT ALLEY WITHIN BLOCK 401, ORIGINAL CITY,TH
CHEYENNE, WYOMING (LOCATED BETWEEN MISSILE DRIVE AND MCCOMB AVENUE
AND BETWEEN W. LINCOLNWAY AND W. 17  STREET).”  Hu Wang, property owner in theTH
near vicinity, spoke in opposition to the vacation; questioned  the vacation process, and expressed
concerns with not rec eiving notice of the proposed action and va cation procedures. He provided
information on the number of tenants who rent property from him in the area and commented that
closing the street would affect the tenants, citing access and safety concerns, particularly during
inclement we ather.  (Clerk’s note :  Dr. Rinne joined the mee ting dur ing comments.) Fur ther
comments from Mr. Wang included the topics of traffic and traffic routes, graffiti and garbage-type
waste disposal in the area, and property values (information and photos distributed and referenced
by Mr. Wang on file with office of City Clerk).  John Rogers, attorney representing applicants
requesting vacation (Robert Smith and Jim Hancock with Wyoming Grand, LLC (involved in the
Microtel hotel project)), addressed notification procedures to adjacent property owners as required
in the vacation application instructions, and stated Mr. Wang  did not receive the mailed wr itten
notification as the Laramie County Assessor’s office inadvertently had an inappropriate address,
utilized by  the ag ent issuing notifica tion notices, on file for Mr . Wang.  H e cla rified notice
concerning the proposed va cation wa s posted on the property  and wa s also adver tised in the
newspaper, and noted Mr. Wang’s presence and participation involving the proposed vacation action
at recent City Council and committee mee tings.  He  spok e in favor of the va cation; reviewed
beneficial aspects surrounding the street vacation; provided a diagram (on file with office of City
Clerk) of the property reflecting the area and access points; discussed street and internal property
lighting, and responded to concerns raised by Mr. Wang involving street lighting, drainage, and
neighboring property values due to the hotel project. Robert Smith, co-owner, Microtel hotel project,
reviewed a brief history of the site involving the  former Frontier Motel and spoke in favor of the
vacation, stating it will help with traffic control in the area and allow for better, safer access to the
property.  Larry Perry, Terrestrial Surveying and Mapping, spoke regarding Mr. Wang’s notification
concern, a dvising L aramie Cou nty’s GI S system us ed f or loc ating p roperty inf ormation h as a
disclaimer on its websit e that it is not liable for the a ccuracy and complete ness of system data.
Reporting for Public Services Committee, Mr. Collins advised there was no recommendation on the
item. Mr. Br own moved to appr ove on third and f inal reading, seconded by Mr. Collins. Upon
inquiry regarding the storm sewer system, City Engineer Ken Lewis provided information generally
on the Public Works Dept. ma intenance prog ram, a nd Mr. Rog ers state d the storm sewe r
infrastructure ha d r ecently be en in spected b y the  City and is o n th e sc hedule fo r p eriodic
maintenance and commented that the existing inlets may be replaced during the hotel construction
project.  Discussion included the following topics: Drainage; storm sewer maintenance schedule;
location of paving of Taco John’s Ice and Events Center parking lot and curb tie-ins (information
provided by Todd Feezer, Parks a nd Recre ation Dept.); a rea development improve ments and
maintenance re sponsibilities; raising  of stree t “cr own” on W. 17  St.; s afety; proposed re -th
development of the area (i.e. new hotel project); access and ligh ting issues; street encroachment
involving Mr. Smith’s residence, and snow plowing of streets during winter months to maintain
accessibility. During discussion and upon inquiry, Larry Gallagher, Summit Engineering, reviewed
drainage development plans for the Microtel property; Mr. Lewis provided information concerning
the stree t encr oachment, and Martin Matsen, D evelopment Di rector, revie wed c urrent zoni ng
designation (CB) a nd allowable use s for prope rty located nor th of the Microte l hotel site.  J ohn
Fafoutakis commented on maintenance schedules of storm sewers in the community. Discussion
continued on drainage and street lighting pursuant to a memorandum issued by Mr. Matsen (on file
with office of City Clerk).  Moti on carried.  Voting  “yes” – a ll members of the g overning body
present with the exception of Mr. Laybourn voting “no”. (#3826)

OF LAND DESCRIBED AS A TRACT OF LAND SITUATED IN A PORTION OF THE NW¼

“AN ORDINANCE ANNEXING TO THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, PARCELS
OF LAND DESCRIBED AS THE W ½ OF TRACT 10, AIRPORT VALLEY TRACTS, LARAMIE
COUNTY, WY (AKA 5905 CROW ROAD).”  Mr. Collins moved to approve on third a nd final
reading, seconded by Ms. Broyles.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing
body present. (#3828)
“AN ORDINANCE VACATING THE ALLEY IN BLOCK ONE AND THE ALLEY IN
BLOCK 2 OF OSTDI EK SUBD IVISION, CHEYEN NE, WYOMI NG (LOCATED EAST OF
WILLS ROAD, BETWEEN CHARLES AND CHEYENNE STREETS).”  Larry Perry, Terrestrial
Surveying and Mapping, provided information concerning the vacation request, stating the alley is
not used nor is it maintained by the City; advised the lots next to the alley are owned by the person
requesting vacation; indicated the frontage of each lot has off-street parking, and explained why alley
access from the lot s (currently fenc ed) is not nee ded.  Mr. Collins advised there  was no
recommendation on the item from Public Services Committee.  Mr. Collins moved to approve on
second reading, seconded by Mr. Brown.  Comment followed on the ability of the neighborhood to
protect the proposed vacated area relative to vandalism and graffiti.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes”
– all members of the governing body present.
Following a five minute recess, Mayor Spiker reconvened the meeting at 7:55 p.m.
“AN ORDINANCE AMEND ING TI TLE 17, Z ONING, CHAPTER 17.08, Z ONING
DISTRICTS ES TABLISHED AND SECTI ON 17.08.040 CHEYEN NE CI TY MAP OF TH E
CHEYENNE CITY CODE 2002, BY CHANGING THE ZONING CLASSIFICATION FROM LR-2
LOW-DENSITY RESI DENTIAL-DEVELOPING DI STRICT TO NB NEI GHBORHOOD
BUSINESS DISTRICT FOR THE SOUTH 130-FEET OF THE WEST 100-FEET OF TRACT 2,
SKYLINE TRACTS, CHEYENNE, WYOMING (LOCATED AT THE NORTHEAST CORNER
OF THE INTERSECTION OF STOREY BOULEVARD AND SYCAMORE ROAD).”   Dr. Rinne
declared a conflict of interest due to personal property ownership in the vicinity and vacated Council
Chambers.  Debb Roden, Woodhouse Law Office, representing Heather and Bob Parry, owners of
the property proposed to be re-zoned, reviewed adjacent property uses, and past and proposed future
developments in the area.  She  reviewed PlanCheyenne criteria, explaining the property meets long-
term growth plans and principles relative to redevelopment of an existing structure, and advised the
proposed property use (limited retail business) will remain residential in appearance and will serve
as a buffer between commercial and residential uses.  She submitted letters in support of the zone
change (on file with the office of City Clerk).  Bob Parry, property owner, spoke in favor of the zone
change; provided information on proposed retail use of the property; reviewed current growth and
development changes in the area which he believes support the zone change, and discussed recent
annexation of the prope rty.  April Br immer Kunz, Br ent Kunz, Katharine Kunz, and Sharon
Knudson, nearby residents, spoke in opposition to the zone change and its effect on the residential
neighborhood.  Topics pre sented and discussed included: Visual a esthetics; protections afforded
through zoning and concern with spot zoning; water and sewer access/costs for properties in the area
that may be annexed in the future; overall future of the vicinity as well as the whole area from Storey
east to the Mustang Ridge subdivision relative to commercial vs. residential uses; traffic concerns;
consistency regarding zoning decisions; maintaining residential integrity of the area; restrictions for
types of uses that will be allowed if re-zoning is approved; area developments, and comments that
the proposed property use does not meet PlanCheyenne criteria (copy of excerpt from PlanCheyenne
submitted and on fi le with office of City  Clerk).  Spea king in f avor of  the zone chang e and
supporting small business endeavors were:  Tim Francois, Jackson’s Sports Bar & Grill; Brandon
Bailey, local artist, and residents David McWain, Theresa Hanson, and Jennifer Jacobson.  Ms.
Roden responded to comments made c oncerning spot zoning, a nd other similar propose d zone
changes within the City that have not been approved.  John Fafoutakis requested that certain types
of businesses be prevented from operating in the area with Mayor Spiker clarifying that regulations
are in place to prevent adult type businesses from operating unless first approved by the governing
body. Paula Qualls, Planning Commission member, commented on a similar past situation involving
a zone change for a business that was denie d by the Commission, and spot zoni ng.  Mr. Collins
advised there was no recommendation on the item from Public Service s Committee. Mr. Brown
moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Discussion included the following
topics: Spot z oning; de finition of neig hborhood; neig hborhood businesses; propose d future
development ( townhomes) north of t he ar ea; pr otecting exis ting ne ighborhoods; future a rea
development t hat ma y inc lude re sidential a nd c ommercial us es, a nd Pla nCheyenne g uidelines.
Motion failed (due to a tie vote).  Voting “yes” – Mr. Brown, Mr. Segrave, Mr. Valdez and Mayor
Spiker.  Voting “no” - Ms. Broyles, Ms. Case, Mr. Collins, and Mr. Laybourn.

Dr. Rinne returned to Council Chambers.

“yes” – all members of the governing body present with the exception of Dr. Rinne voting “no”.

Agreement between Laramie County School District No. 1 and the City of Cheyenne, Office
of Youth Alternatives, for counseling services to elementary and secondary age children who are
attending the PEP/TRC Center.  Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr. Segrave moved to approve
in an amount not to exceed $42,765.00, seconded by Mr. Collins.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” –
all members of the governing body present.  (#5302)
Agreement between Laramie County School District No. 1 and the City of Cheyenne, Office
of Youth Alternatives, for family counseling for school referred students who are in I.E.P.s.  Mr.
Segrave moved to approve in an amount not to exceed $8,740.00, seconded by Mr. Valdez.  Motion
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.  (#5303)
 
Agreement between Laramie County School District No. 1 and the City of Cheyenne, Office
of Youth Alternatives, f or family counseling for sc hool referred students/families.  Mr. Seg rave
moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Valdez.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the
governing body present.  (#5304)
Contract between the City of Cheyenne, Metropolitan Planning Organization, and Quinby
Clune Design for the “Cheyenne Wayfinding Plan Phase II”.  Mr. Segrave moved to approve in an
amount n ot t o e xceed $ 34,500.00, se conded b y Mr . Brown. U pon inqu iry, T om M ason, MPO
Director, provided MPO budget and staffing level information and explained financial assistance for
the project by the Cheyenne Area Convention and Visitors Bureau, purpose of the signage and that
the wayfinding signs will be specific to the Cheyenne community.  He also provided information on
Phase I of the project, state highway sign requirements and elements that will be involved in this next
phase of the project, including sign design, location, content and cost estimates.  He stated once the
next phase has been completed, a plan will be provided to the governing body for proposed approval,
and indicated future funding has not yet been identified.  Discussion followed on hiring a consultant
vs. utilizing city planning staff, ne ed for quality community signage, and funding.  Mr . Mason
clarified that MPO planning funds can not be used for the signs.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all
members of the governing body present with the exception of Mr. Laybourn and Dr. Rinne voting
“no”.  (#5305)

Planning Organization and Ayres Associates for the Missile Drive Corridor Plan -- referred to

School, (SRTS) Johnson-Goins Corridor Construction (1% Specific Purpose Sales and Use Tax)  --

Engineering Dept., and Utah Pacific Bridge and Steel Ltd., for moving and storing bridge girders
(1% Specific Purpose Sales and Use Tax) -- referred to Finance Committee.
CONSIDERATION OF BIDS:
Consideration of Bid #M-4-09 for purchasing a bulk ice control product for the Public Works
Department for use on city streets, from Desert Mountain Corp., Riverton, WY (2003-2006 and
2007-2010 1% Sales Tax).  Mr. Segrave moved to approve in an amount not to exceed $341,400.00,
seconded by Mr. Collins. Comments followed on environmental concerns involving the product,
whether the product affects concrete, and product effectiveness.  Upon inquiry, Mike Abel, Public
Works Director, stated the product has been used for 8-9 years, with the exception of last year (lack
of a storage site due to Street & Alley shop relocation); that Street & Alley staff have advised the
product provides better effective results than a sand/salt mixture, and that the City’s on-line “Request
Partner” program tracks communications received from the public and that he would follow-up to
see if any comments had been receive d pertaining to product concerns.  Motion carr ied.  Voting
“yes” – all members of the governing body present with the exception of Ms. Case and Mr. Laybourn
voting “no”.  
Consideration of Bid #R-5-09 for furnishing and installing one new stationary solid waste
Knuckleboom Crane for the Transfer Station, from Northshore Manufacturing, Two Harbors, MN.
Mr. Segrave moved to approve in an amount not to exceed $146,435.00, seconded by Mr. Brown.
Comment followed on communication received from a bidder expressing concerns that purchasing
procedures had not been followed.  Upon inquiry, Rudy Apodaca, Purchasing Director, provided
information regarding the non-recommended bidder’s concern with procedures, and advised other
Wyoming municipalities are also receiving competitor complaints on bid procedures which have
been utilized for a number of years, and that the current state of the economy may be a factor relative
to complaints.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.
[CA] Consideration of Bid #M-02-09 for a contract between the City of Cheyenne and Edward
Hawley, LLC, Torrington, WY, for the Deming/Walterscheid Blvd. Reconstruction Project (1999-
2002 1% Sales Tax) (SLIB Grant)  -- referred to Finance Committee.
[CA] Consideration of Bid #R-6-09 from Western Plains Machinery, Casper, WY, for furnishing
one new three wheel street sweeper, and to trade a 1999 Johnson 4000 street sweeper, for the Street
and Alley Dept. (2007-2010 1% Sales Tax) -- referred to Finance Committee.
[CA] Consideration of Bid #R-7-09 from Tom Scott, Cheyenne, WY, for the surplus sale of bulk
wood, for the Forestry Dept. -- referred to Finance Committee.
LICENSES/PERMITS/APPLICATIONS:
[CA] Transfer of ownership for a retail liquor license application filed with the City Clerk’s Office
for ENI, Inc. D/b/a Eagle’s Nest, 1101 W. Lincolnway, Cheyenne, WY.  (License currently held by
Eagle’s Nest, Inc. d/b/a Eagle’s Nest, 1101 W. Lincolnway, Cheyenne, WY). Motion (per consent
agenda) carried.
APPOINTMENTS:
Reappointment of Paul Frauendienst to the Building Code Board of Appeals and Elizabeth
Davis to the Greater Cheyenne Arts Advisory Council.  Mr. Collins moved to approve, seconded by
Mr. Segrave.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.
Appointment of Richard Shanor to the Community Action of Laramie County Board, Robert
Kanard to the Greenway Advisory Committee, Carrisa Pollard to the Planning Commission, Nicole
Novotny to t he Board of Adjustments, Jackie Smith to the Personnel Commission, and Phyllis
Sherard to t he Housing and Community Development Advisory Board.  M r. Collins moved to
approve, seconded by Mr. Brown.  April Brimmer Kunz commented on the appointment of Mr.
Shanor.  Concern was expressed with approving all of the propose d appointees en masse as one
agenda item with Mayor Spiker advising a motion to divide the question could be offered.  Motion
carried.  Voting  “yes” – a ll members of the g overning body present with the exception of Mr.
Laybourn voting “no”.
Following inquiry, no other City business was presented by the public.  Mr. Brown moved
to adjourn, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing
body present with the exception of Ms. Broy les, Ms. Case and Mr . Laybourn voting “no”.  T he
meeting was officially adjourned at 9:34 p.m.
 Submitted by,
 
 Carol Intlekofer, MMC
 City Clerk

Valdez.  Dr.  Ri nne dec lared a c onflict o f i nterest a nd v acated C ouncil Ch ambers.  Di ckey

read a  s tatement f rom M s. B rimmer-Kunz r elaying he r o pposition t o r econsideration of t he
ordinance a s w ell a s t he o rdinance r equesting r e-zoning.  B ob P arry, applicant r equesting t he
zone change, spoke in favor of the re-zoning request and briefly described why the request had
been submitted; discussed PlanCheyenne criteria and zoning issues, and expressed concern with
required public readi ngs o f t he o rdinance t itle.  S haron K nudson an d Bruce M ilmont s poke
against t he motion and t he o rdinance.  C omments included property o wner’s knowledge o f t he
residential zone when the property was purchased; commercial use(s) infringing on r esidential
neighborhood, and tr affic issues.  D onn Edmunds commented on oth er areas of the c ommunity
involving m ixed r esidential a nd c ommercial u ses.  Discussion f ollowed o n c ity d evelopment
requirements, P lanCheyenne d ocument, c hallenges i nvolving g rowth a nd z oning i ssues, a nd
further review of issues surrounding the re-zoning request. Upon inquiry, Deputy City Attorney
Erik Rasmussen a dvised he felt th e or dinance has met p ublic r eading r equirements.  M otion to
reconsider c arried.  V oting “yes” –  a ll m embers o f t he g overning b ody p resent in  C ouncil
Chambers with the exception of Mr. Collins and Mr. Laybourn voting “no”.  Ms. Broyles moved
to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. Brown.  Ms. Broyles moved to postpone for two
weeks ( to O ct. 2 7, 2 008), s econded b y M r. V aldez.  Co mment followed on  r econsideration
procedures and ordinance readings.  Motion to postpone carried.  Voting “yes” - all members of
the g overning bo dy p resent i n C ouncil C hambers w ith t he e xception o f M r. L aybourn vo ting
“no”.  Following announcement of the vote, Dr. Rinne returned to Council Chambers.
Mr. V aldez, indicating he had voted on  th e p revailing side on  a n or dinance a cted u pon
(approved o n 3  reading) at t he September 22, 2008 meeting concerning vacation of McCombrd
Ave, moved to reconsider the ordinance, seconded by Mr. Laybourn.  U pon request, City Clerk
Carol Intlekofer read the following agenda t itle o f t he o rdinance pro posed t o be reco nsidered:
“AN ORDINANCE ON THIRD READING VACATING MCCOMB AVENUE BETWEEN W.
LINCOLNWAY AND W. 17  STREET AND THE 16-FOOT ALLEY WITHIN BLOCK 401,TH
ORIGINAL CITY, CHEYENNE, WYOMING (LOCATED BETWEEN MISSILE DRIVE AND
MCCOMB AVENUE A ND BETWEEN W. L INCOLNWAY A ND W. 17  ST REET).”  HuTH
Wang, property owner in the near vicinity of McComb Ave., spoke in opposition to the vacation,
questioned the vacation process and procedures, and extensively discussed various concerns he
had involving vacation of the street.  M otion to r econsider failed.  V oting “no” - all members of
the governing body present.
There b eing n o f urther b usiness to c ome b efore th e g overning b ody, th e m eeting w as
officially adjourned at 12:05 p.m.
 Submitted by,
 
 Carol Intlekofer, MMC

within the building, and that no other uses shall be allowed by administrative or board approval,
seconded by Mr. Pierson.  Ms. Brimmer-Kunz commented that if the zone change was approved,
she s upported t he m otion t o p lace c onditional z one l imitations, a nd q uestioned ho w t he
conditions would be applied to a property deed.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Matsen provided information
on a dministrative “f lagging” o f t he c onditions, i ncluding a notation o n t he z one m ap, a nd
advised he was unsure how a condition would be handled on deeds.  He clarified notification of
legal zoning u ses o f property to real estate agents is not required and t ypically a buyer o r t heir
agent m akes i nquiry.  C omments f ollowed on  th e conditional u se r estrictions; n eighborhood
protection wh ile f itting wi th t he pro perty o wners’ n eeds, an d co ncern wi th pre cedent s etting.
Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present in Council
Chambers wi th th e e xception of  M r. L aybourn v oting “ no”. B rief d iscussion followed on  s pot
zones, P lanCheyenne d ocument, a nd d evelopment. M ain m otion as amended c arried. V oting
“yes” – all members of the governing body present in Council Chambers with the exception of
Ms. Case, Mr. Collins and Mr. Laybourn voting “no”. 
Following a ten minute recess, Mayor Spiker reconvened the meeting at 10:45 p.m.  D r.
Rinne returned to Council Chambers.

Authorizing t he ex ecution o f a co ntract f or pur chase o f real  pro perty l ocated at  1621

for t he development o f do wntown public parking.  May or Spiker declared a co nflict o f interest
(nearby p roperty o wner i nterests), relinquished d uties o f t he c hair t o C ouncil V ice-President
Broyles, and vacated Council Chambers.  John Fafoutakis questioned proposed parking rates and
public availability, an d co mmented o n co ncerns wi th graf fiti, as  ex perienced in t he do wntown
parking structure, and adequate utilization of the new parking lot to uniformly benefit businesses

Christian C herek, D irector, D owntown D evelopment A uthority; Jerry I nnis, o wner, P izzeria
Venti; Gl oria M ilmont, resi dent, an d Gl en Garret t, arch itect, s poke i n favor o f t he pro posed
parking lot.  Topics discussed included: lack of dedicated parking on the east side of downtown;
parking lot benefit to existing and potential new businesses, downtown customers and residents;
2006 D DA parking study; past p arking studies;  D DA goal t o r ecruit d ining a nd e ntertainment
operations on 17  St.; effects lack of parking has had on downtown businesses, and developmentth
and character of the downtown area.  Mark Shubert spoke in opposition of property acquisition,
expressing h is b elief t hat s ervice (privately o wned) parking lots s hould b e ut ilized.  R eporting
for Finance Committee, Mr. Segrave moved to approve in an amount not to exceed $700,000.00,
seconded by Mr. Collins.  Comments included: importance of downtown, improving p arking
availability, previous parking studies indicating t he need for more parking areas, and additional
parking co ntributing t o economic development o f do wntown.  M otion carried.  Vo ting “yes” –
all members of the governing body present in Council Chambers with the exception of Ms. Case
voting “no”. Following a nnouncement of th e vote, Ms. Broyles r eturned d uties of th e c hair to
Mayor Spiker, who returned to Council Chambers.  (#5318)

Council Chambers.  Ka tharine Kunz, speaking in opposition to t he zone change, submitted and
discussed a petition (on file with t he City Clerk’s o ffice) signed by 45 individuals, t he majority
of whom reside within the general neighborhood area, who are opposed to the zone change, and
read the introduction of the petition.  Also speaking in opposition to the zone change were June
Lopez Wood, Terri Foley, Jack Knudson, Gloria Milmont, April Brimmer Kunz and Joe Kasper.
Topics of concern presented included: Zone change resulting in deterioration and disruption of
the neighborhood’s residential integrity; commercial development on north side of Sto rey Blvd.;
traffic an d s afety; Pl anCheyenne gui delines f or ar ea gro wth; i nconsistency o f zo ne ch anges
involving co mmercial uses in es tablished res idential n eighborhoods; ch anging t he res idential
character o f t he n eighborhood; s upport fo r a  M ixed U se R esidential (MUR) z one instead o f a
Neighborhood Business zone; past actions on the proposed ordinance by the governing body and
Planning Commission; pro visions o f Wyo. St at. §15-1-601(d)(iv) and 15-1-603(a); petition t hat
had been submitted; governing body discretion with reference to potential arbitrary a nd
capricious actions; previous amendment to the ordinance involving conditional zoning and effect
on po tential future pr operty o wner(s); prev ious act ions b y t he go verning b ody involving o ther
similar z one c hange r equests, a nd the e ffect o f “block bu sting” a nd p recedence s etting i f t he
zone change was approved.  B randon Bailey spoke in favor o f the zone change, expressing his
support f or s mall b usinesses a nd th e op portunity th e b usiness w ould p rovide l ocal a rtists to
display and sell items.  D ebb Roden, Woodhouse Law Office, attorney representing the property
owners, spoke in favor of the zone change and responded to the concerns that had been presented
involving t raffic, PlanCheyenne, language contained in state statutes relating t o zoning, and t he
previously approved amendment c oncerning a conditional zoning provision that would limit t he
types of uses al lowed o n the property.  Ms. Ro den provided information o n proposed business
hours and the types of items that would be available for sale; property owners’ compromise (i.e.
conditional zo ning) e fforts, an d ref erenced a  prev ious co mmunication s he h ad s ubmitted
involving filing of the conditional zoning information with the County real estate office to assist
with title searches applicable to any future property owners of the site.  Ro n Pinther commented
on a recent zone change request he had submitted that was denied by the governing body; spoke
in fa vor o f implementing a  n eighborhood mediation p rocess fo r z one c hange r equests o f t his
type, a nd s tated h is s upport g enerally fo r t his p roposed z one c hange. R eporting fo r P ublic
Services C ommittee, M r. C ollins a dvised there was no  r ecommendation o n t he i tem f rom t he
Committee. Mr. Brown moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave.
Comment followed on examples of other small businesses located within r esidential
environments, state statute phrase of “arbitrary and capricious” relative to the conditional zoning
amendment, and whether a possible MUR zone had been reviewed by staff.  U pon inquiry, Bob
Nicholas, Acting City Attorney, stated t he phrase “arbitrary and capricious” is a legal t erm and
any c hallenge t o C ouncil m embers’ a ctions w ould be  r esolved t hrough a  c ourt o f l aw, a nd
advised t hat t he petition submitted appeared t o not invoke a “super majority” vote requirement
(per s tate s tatute) as  it h ad n ot b een repr esented, o nly inferred, t o Co uncil t hat t he s ignatures
received constituted 20% of the adjacent property owners located within 140 feet of the property
involved.  Mart in M atsen, Dev elopment Di rector, co nfirmed a M UR zo ne di strict h ad b een
discussed during the process and that a larger property coverage area was required, and t he
applicants d id no t p ursue a  r equest for that z one t ype.  D iscussion followed a nd included t he
conditional zoning amendment previously approved, which limits the property’s commercial use
and af fords s ome pro tection t o t he neighborhood; s pot zo ning; pro tection o f es tablished
residential neighborhoods; future uses of properties north of Storey; commercial business s ites
available in th e c ommunity, a nd c oncern w ith c onditional z oning limitations g enerally.  M otion
carried. V oting “ye s” – M r. B rown, M s. B royles, M r. P ierson, M r. S egrave, M r. V aldez a nd
Mayor Spiker.  V oting “no” – Ms. Case, Mr. Collins and Mr. Laybourn voting “no”. Following
announcement of the vote, Dr. Rinne returned to Council Chambers.  (#3832)

requesting co nsideration o f addi tional f unding f or m unicipalities duri ng t he s tate’s b udget
process to assist with ongoing expenses and revenue shortfalls, and questioned the state’s fiscal
responsibilities t o he lp f und c ities a nd towns.  M ayor S piker p rovided i nformation o n t he
purpose of the letter, noted the effect of reduced m onies to municipalities due t o the food sales
tax exemption, and advised the Wyoming Association of Municipalities had adopted resolutions
at i ts s tate convention i n s upport o f pursui ng addi tional s tate f unding t o m ake m unicipalities
“whole” with regard to food sales tax backfill.

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
December 8, 2008
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date a t 6:00
p.m. in Council Chambers.  Pr esent were: MAYOR - Jack R. Spiker; COUNCIL MEMBERS -
Mr. B rown, Ms. B royles, Ms. Case, Mr. Coll ins, Mr. Laybourn, Mr. Pierson, Dr . Rinne, Mr.
Segrave and Mr. Valdez.  The pledge of allegiance was recited.
 
Also present: Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk; Barb Dorr, City Treasurer, and Bob Nicholas,
Acting City Attorney.
Consent Agenda. (All agenda items listed with the desig nation of [CA]  are considered to
be routine items by  the g overning body and will  be enacted by one motion.  There  will be no
separate discussion on these items unle ss a  member of the governing body so requests and
support by two other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be
considered in its normal sequence  on the agenda.)  Upon request by Mr. Laybourn, and pursuant
to support received by Ms. Case and Mr. Collins, May or Spiker announced agenda items #27(a)
and #28(a ) would be removed f rom the Consent Ag enda.  Mr . Colli ns moved to approve
remaining Consent Agenda items, seconded by Mr. Brown.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all
members of the governing body with the exception of Mr. Laybourn voting “no”.
 [CA] MINUTES FROM REGUL AR MEETING OF  THE GOV ERNING B ODY ON

“AN ORDINANCE AMENDING SECTION 6.12.020, RAB IES TAGS, OF CHAPTER
6.12, DOG AND CAT REGISTRATION, AND SECTION 6.16.020, RABIES VACCINATION
REQUIRED, OF CHA PTER 6.16, RABI ES CONTROL , OF T ITLE 6, ANIMALS, OF THE
CODE OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING.”  John Fafoutakis spoke in support of the
ordinance.  Reporting  for Public Service s Committee, Mr. Seg rave moved to approve  on third
and fina l rea ding, se conded by Mr. Pierson.  Mr. Seg rave moved to amend by  substitut e,
seconded by Mr. Pierson.  I t was advised the  substitu te addresses r egistration concerns. Karon
Volk spoke in favor of the substitute.  Upon inquiry , Bob Ma son, City Staff Attorne y, clarified
that lang uage conta ined in Section 6.12.020(A), r eferring to animal  registration for
Cheyenne/Laramie County, meant that registration acquired through local veterinary offices will
pertain to both city  and county residents.  Discussion topics included: County  Board of Health’s
approval of t he ra bies vac cination being  administered e very 3 y ears as an option; curr ent
ordinance history; input on proposed ordinanc e changes from local veterinarians, dog breeders
and the Dir ector of the Chey enne A nimal Control/S helter; ra bies vac cination, registration and
regulation; due diligence efforts to provide notification of proposed changes to the community;
concern with potential rabie s outbreak in the c ommunity, and purpose  of the ordina nce.  John
Rogers expressed support for  the ordinanc e change, and indica ted his belief that, due to local
media cove rage, ade quate publicity  on the proposed or dinance had bee n provided. Mr.
Fafoutakis commented in support of the proposed substitute, and Ms. Volk advised the proposed
ordinance doesn’ t chang e re porting or animal re gistration require ments.  Further discussion
continued o n pet owners’ r equirement t o u pdate inf ormation f or yearly re gistration w ith the ir
veterinarian’s office, and language pertaining to Section 6.12.020(A) previously referenced, with
further information provided by Attorney Mason.  Mr. Brown moved to amend the substitute by
striking the words “Cheyenne/Laramie County” from Section 6.12.020(A), and inserting “City of
Cheyenne”, seconded by Mr. Collins.  Upon  inquiry, Acting City Attorney Bob N icholas stated
the inclusion of L aramie Count y was more  for infor mational purposes, not a ma ndatory
requirement, and, during discussion, Attorney Mason provided further information relative to the
reference to Laramie County.  Discussion followed on the pr oposed amendment.  It was advised
that currently the County provides pet owners the option  of three year vs. annual vaccinations.
Motion to amend failed (due to a tie vote).  Voting “yes” – Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Collins,
Dr. Rinne and Mr . Segrave.  Voting “ no” – Ms. Case, Mr. Laybourn, Mr. Pierson, Mr. V aldez
and Mayor Spiker. Discussion continued on the proposed substitute and included: Purpose of the
ordinance; review committee composition and involvement during ordinance compilation; public
representation; involvement by local veterinarians, and content areas of the ordinance applicable
to vaccinations, reporting and registration.  Upon inquiry,  Mr. Mason prov ided vaccination and
registration information.  Motion to amend by  substitute carried.  Voting “ yes” - all members of
the governing body with the exception of Ms. Case voting “no”.  Discussion continued on the
main motion as amended a nd included: Vaccination certification time frames; yearly registration
requirement an d any  aff iliated fee s that may  be asse ssed by  veter inarians; Animal Shelter’ s
involvement with registration, and concerns with wild animals within the City that may be rabies
carriers.  Mr. Mason explained information contained on rabies/registration animal tags as issued
by loc al ve terinarians.  M ain m otion a s a mended b y su bstitute c arried. Vo ting “ yes” – a ll
members of the governing body with the exception of Ms. Cas e and Mr. Laybourn voting “no”.

LEASES/CONTRACTS/LEGAL:
Contract between the City of Cheyenne and WINhealth Partners for city employee health
coverage. Jas on Sa nchez, Pr esident o f t he Che yenne Pub lic Em ployees Association, sp oke in
support of the c ontract on behalf of  the Association.  Mr. Seg rave moved to approve, seconded
by Mr . B rown.  D iscussion f ollowed o n he alth ins urance c ost in creases f or re tirees;
WINHealth’s HMO (Health Maintenance Organization) plan structure, and health coverage plan
pricing and lac k of  informa tion, described as  proprietary and conf idential, received during the
bidding pr ocess.  Che ryl H ageman, HRH, c onsultant, a dvised it  is n ot u ncommon fo r s ome
insurance entities to not disclose pricing  information betwe en the c ompany and hea lth care
providers.  U pon inquiry , Rich  Wiederspahn, Dire ctor of H uman Resourc es, provided
information on communication efforts made to advise employees of the proposed insurance plan
and rates.  Di scussion continued on premiums and deductible rates.  B eth Wasson, WINHealth
Partners, provided further information on non-disclosure of fees that are established b etween
health providers and WINHealth; HMO plan structure a nd local phy sician participation; patient
responsibilities involving co-payments, and usual and c ustomary fee schedules under tra ditional
health care plans.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the g overning body with the
exception of Ms. Case and Mr. Laybourn voting “no”. (#5322)

CONSIDERATION OF BIDS:
Consideration of Bid #M-7- 09 for a contract between the City of Cheyenne, Engineering
Dept., and Tarco, Inc., Arvada, CO, for the Henderson Basin Flood Control Improvement Project
(2007-2010 1% Sales Tax).  J ohn Fafoutakis expressed c oncern with the item being  placed on
the Consent Agenda due to the amount of the bid.  Mr. Seg rave moved to approve in an amount
not to exceed $2,888,909.58, seconded by Mr. Brown.  Upon inquiry, Ken Lewis, City Engineer,
provided project location information; advised overall future effect of the project will result in
reduction of the specia l flood hazard ele vation in the Ba sin reg ion, parti cularly during  storm
events, and confirmed savings in the bid total of approximately $1 million.  Du ring comments
involving the Consent Ag enda process, Mr. Collins and Mr. B rown each raised points of orde r
that comments were not germane to the motion on the floor.  Mayor Spiker ruled that comments
could continue but s hould pertain to the motion. Following fur ther c omments on Consent
Agenda procedures, motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. (#5323)

ANNOUNCEMENTS/REPORTS/MOTIONS:
Announcement was made  that due to t he Christmas holiday, the next reg ular meeting of
the Governing B ody wi ll b e he ld M onday, D ecember 2 2, 20 08 a t 3: 00 p. m. i n Ci ty Cou ncil
Chambers.
Announcement was made that the Cheyenne City Council will hold a public hearing to
discuss the sale of the City-owned real property located at 700 West Fox  Farm Road, Cheyenne,
Wyoming to Attention Homes, I nc. which sal e will benefit the e conomic deve lopment of the
City of Cheyenne, pursuant to Wyo. Stat. § 15-1-112(b)(i)(D).  The estimated appraised value of
said property to be sold is $184,000 and will be sold to Attention Homes, Inc. for $2,000.  This
land will provide additional space to fa cilitate the expansion and re development of  Attention
Homes, I nc.  U nder o ther Ci ty b usiness, M r. Pi erson n oted th ere was n o d ate in cluded in  the
public hear ing a nnouncement.  City  Clerk Carol I ntlekofer stated her  belief the  hear ing w as
proposed to be he ld at the December  22  meeting  (3:00 p.m.) and Ma yor Spiker stated thend
matter would be reviewed with legal staff to determine whether re-advertising (i.e. rescheduling)
of the hearing date and time may be required for public notification.
Announcement was made of a public hearing to be held December 22, 2008 at 3:00 p.m.
in City Council Chambers fo r a restaurant liquor lice nse application filed with the City  Clerk’s
Office for Yu Zheng d/b/a Golden House Restaurant, 1651 Carey Ave., Ste. 8, Cheyenne, WY.
Announcement was made of the a ppointment of Tony  Adams to the Personne l
Commission, Mark Har dee to the F ire Civil Service Commissi on, Michael L emke to the
Housing and Community Development Advisory Council and Jody Malm and B everly Bowman
to the Board of Adjustment, to be confirmed December 22, 2008.
OTHER CITY BUSINESS:
John Fa foutakis expressed his conc erns with the volume of  state-wide  alcohol re lated
offenses and affiliated law enforcement time and expense.
City Councilwoman-elect Amber Ash commented on inaudible portions of City Council
meeting streaming video a rchives, due to persons not spea king directly into microphones, and
suggested a reminder be made at meetings to assist in improving the sound quality.
There b eing no further business to come before the governing body, the meeting was
officially adjourned at 7:51 p.m.

Office of City Clerk
December 22, 2008
Due to the Christmas holiday season, the regular meeting of the Governing Body of the
City o f Cheyenne was re-scheduled t o begin at  3:00 p. m. in Council Chambers.  Pr esent were:
MAYOR - J ack R. Sp iker; COUNCIL MEMBERS -  Mr. B rown, M s. B royles, M s. C ase, M r.
Collins, Mr. Laybourn, Mr. Pierson, Mr. Segrave and Mr. Valdez. Absent: Dr. Rinne. The pledge
of allegiance was recited.
 
Also present: Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk; Barb Dorr, City Treasurer, and Bob Nicholas,
Acting City Attorney.
